\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(x_{\star}[i],x_{1}[i],\dotsc,x[t];a[i])-\mathbb{E}h(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A})\right|\leq(i)+(ii)+(iii),

(97)

where,

\displaystyle(i)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(x_{\star}[i],x_{1}[i],\dotsc,x[t];a[i])-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(x_{\star}[i],x_{1}^{(D)}[i],\dotsc,x^{(D)}_{t}[i];a[i])\right|

\displaystyle(ii)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(x_{\star}[i],x_{1}^{(D)}[i],\dotsc,x^{(D)}_{t}[i];a[i])-\mathbb{E}[h(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{t};\mathsf{A})]\right|

\displaystyle(iii)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\left|\mathbb{E}[h(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{t};\mathsf{A})]-\mathbb{E}[h(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A})]\right|.

We consider each of the terms above. From Claim
2
and (
96
), we conclude that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plimsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}(i)

\displaystyle=0.

Since we assume that Theorem
1
holds for OAMP algorithms with polynomial matrix denoisers,

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(ii)

\displaystyle=0.

Finally, Claim
1
implies that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}(iii)

\displaystyle=0.

We let
N\rightarrow\infty
and
D\rightarrow\infty
in (
97
) and use the results above to conclude that:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}h(x_{\star}[i],x_{1}[i],\dotsc,x[t];a[i])

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}h(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}),

as desired. To finish the proof of the lemma, we need to prove Claim
1
and Claim
2
.

Convergence of State Evolution Random Variables (Proof of Claim
1
)

Our goal is to show that:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})\overset{W_{2}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A})\quad\text{ as $D\rightarrow\infty$}\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N},

(98)

We show this by induction. As our induction hypothesis, we assume that (
98
) holds for some
t\in\mathbb{N}
and show that:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t+1}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})\overset{W_{2}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t+1};\mathsf{A})\quad\text{ as $D\rightarrow\infty$}.

To this end, we consider a test function
h:\mathbb{R}^{t+2}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
which satisfies the smoothness requirement in (
96
) and verify that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[h(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t+1}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[h(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t+1};\mathsf{A})].

(99)

Recall from Definition
3
that the joint distribution of the state evolution random variables is given by:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})\sim\pi,\quad\mathsf{X}_{i}=\mathsf{\beta}_{i}\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\mathsf{Z}_{i},\quad\mathsf{X}_{i}^{(D)}=\mathsf{\beta}_{i}^{(D)}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\mathsf{Z}_{i}^{(D)}\quad\forall\;i\;\in\;[t+1],

where
(\mathsf{Z}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{Z}_{t+1})
and
(\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{Z}_{t+1}^{(D)})
are Gaussian random vectors, sampled independently of
(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})
:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{Z}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{Z}_{t+1})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Sigma_{t+1}\right),\quad(\mathsf{Z}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{Z}_{t+1}^{(D)})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Sigma^{(D)}_{t+1}\right).

In the above equations, coefficients
(\beta_{i})_{i\in[t+1]}
and
(\beta_{i}^{(D)})_{i\in[t+1]}
are given by:

\displaystyle\beta_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\mathsf{F}_{i}]\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})],\quad\beta_{i}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\mathsf{F}_{i}^{(D)}]\left(\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi_{i}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})]-\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi_{i}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\right)\;\;\forall\;i\;\in\;[t+1],

and the entries of the covariance matrices
{\Sigma}{}_{t+1},\Sigma^{(D)}_{t+1}
are given by:

\displaystyle\left({\Sigma}{}_{t+1}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}{\mathsf{F}}_{i}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}]\cdot\mathrm{Cov}[{\Psi}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}),{\Psi}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})]

\displaystyle+(\mathbb{E}[{\mathsf{F}}_{i}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}{\mathsf{F}}_{i}]\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}])\cdot\mathrm{Cov}[{\Psi}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),{\Psi}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\quad\forall\;i,j\;\in\;[t+1],

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma^{(D)}_{t+1}\right)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{i}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{j}]\cdot\mathrm{Cov}[\Psi^{(D)}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}),\Psi^{(D)}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})]

\displaystyle+(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{i}\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{j}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}{\mathsf{F}}_{i}]\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}])\cdot\mathrm{Cov}[\Psi^{(D)}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),\Psi^{(D)}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\quad\forall\;i,j\;\in\;[t+1],

where
\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu
and
\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu
and:

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}f_{i}(\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{i-1};\mathsf{A}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}f_{i}(\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{i-1};\mathsf{A})-\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{j}f_{i}(\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{i-1};\mathsf{A})]\cdot\mathsf{X}^{(D)}_{j}.

By a weak convergence and uniform integrability argument, (
99
) follows if we can show that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\beta_{i}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\beta_{i},\quad\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\left(\Sigma^{(D)}_{t+1}\right)_{i,j}=\left({\Sigma}{}_{t+1}\right)_{i,j}\quad\forall\;i,j\;\in\;[t+1].

(100)

Indeed, from (
94
) we deduce that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[{\Psi_{t}}(\mathsf{\Lambda})],

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[{\Psi_{t}}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})],

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{Cov}[\Psi^{(D)}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),\Psi^{(D)}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}[{\Psi}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),{\Psi}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda})],

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{Cov}[\Psi^{(D)}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}),\Psi^{(D)}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}[{\Psi}_{i}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}),{\Psi}_{j}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})].

Furthermore, using the induction hypothesis
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})\overset{W_{2}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A})
and the fact that the functions
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}
are continuously differentiable and Lipschitz, we conclude that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{i}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}{\mathsf{F}}{}_{i}],\quad\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{i}\mathsf{F}^{(D)}_{j}]=\mathbb{E}[{\mathsf{F}}{}_{i}{\mathsf{F}}_{j}].

Plugging in these limits into the formulae for
(\beta^{(D)}_{i})_{i\in[t+1]}
and
{\Sigma}{}_{t+1},\Sigma^{(D)}_{t+1}
, immediately yields the desired conclusion (
100
) and hence proves (
99
). This completes the proof of the claim (
98
) by induction.

Convergence of Iterates (Proof of Claim
2
)

We need to show that the iterates generated by the algorithm in (
95
) approximate the iterates generated by the original OAMP algorithm (
93
) in the sense that,

\displaystyle\limsup_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plimsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\bm{x}_{t}-\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

We will shorthand asymptotic approximation statements like the above using the notation:

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}\quad\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}{\bm{x}}_{t}\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

(101)

We will show the above claim using induction. As our induction hypothesis, we will assume that the claim holds for all the iterates generated before step
t
of the OAMP algorithm:

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{s}^{(D)}\quad\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}{\bm{x}}_{s}\quad\forall\;s<t.

(102)

To complete the inductive proof, we need to show that the claim applies to the iterate generated at step
t
:

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}\quad

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}{\bm{x}}_{t}.

Recalling the update equation for
{\bm{x}_{t}}
from (
93
), we have:

\displaystyle{\bm{x}}_{t}=\Psi_{t}(\bm{Y})\cdot f_{t}({\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{x}_{t-1};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}\left(\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\right)\cdot f_{t}({\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{x}_{t-1};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}\left(\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\bm{I}_{N}\right)\left(f_{t}({\bm{x}}_{<t};\bm{a})-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s}f_{t}(\mathsf{X}_{<t}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]{\bm{x}_{s}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}\left(\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})-\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\bm{I}_{N}\right)\left(f_{t}(\bm{x}_{<t}^{(D)};\bm{a})-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s}f_{t}(\mathsf{X}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t-1}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]\bm{x}_{s}^{(D)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:low-degree-approx-div-free-algo}}}}{=}\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}.

In the above display:

1.

The approximation in step (a) relies on the observations:

\displaystyle\limsup_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plimsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|\Psi_{t}(\bm{Y})-\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})\|_{\op}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{|\lambda|\leq K}\left|\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\lambda)-\Psi_{t}(\lambda)\right|\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:weierstrass}}}}{=}0,

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{t}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{t}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{Def. \ref{Def:OAMP_main}}}}{=}0.

2.

The approximation in step (b) uses Claim
1
and the fact that
f_{t}
is Lipschitz to conclude that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s}f_{t}(\mathsf{X}_{1}^{(D)},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t-1}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s}f_{t}({\mathsf{X}_{1}},\dotsc,{\mathsf{X}_{t-1}};\mathsf{A})]=0,

where the last equality follows by recalling that the iterate denoisers satisfy the divergence-free requirement
\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s}f_{t}({\mathsf{X}_{1}},\dotsc,{\mathsf{X}_{t-1}};\mathsf{A})]=0
(recall Definition
3
).

3.

The approximation in step (c) relies on the induction hypothesis (
102
) and the fact that the functions
f_{t}
are Lipschitz.

Hence, (
101
) holds by induction. This concludes the proof of Lemma
5
.
∎

B.2
Proof of Lemma
6

Proof of Lemma
6
.

. Since any polynomial can be written as a linear combination of monomials, it suffices to prove the claims of the lemma when
\Psi(\lambda)=\lambda^{d}\;\forall\;\lambda\;\in\mathbb{R}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof of Claim (1)

We need to show that there exists a sequence of polynomials
(Q_{d})_{d\in\mathbb{N}}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
such that:

\displaystyle\bm{Y}^{d}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}\;

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\;{Q}_{d}(\bm{W})\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}\quad\forall\;d\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

(103)

We will show that (
103
) holds by induction on
d
. We assume that (
103
) holds for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
and show that the claim also holds for
d+1
. Recalling that
\bm{Y}=\theta\cdot\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}\bm{x}_{\star}^{\mathsf{T}}}{N}+\bm{W}
, we have:

\displaystyle\bm{Y}^{d+1}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\left(\theta\cdot\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}\bm{x}_{\star}^{\mathsf{T}}}{N}+\bm{W}\right)\cdot\bm{Y}^{d}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}\left(\theta\cdot\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}\bm{x}_{\star}^{\mathsf{T}}}{N}+\bm{W}\right)\cdot Q_{d}(\bm{W})\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\theta\cdot\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}Q_{d}(\bm{W})\bm{x}_{\star}}{N}+\bm{W}Q_{d}(\bm{W})\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}\theta\cdot\mathbb{E}[Q_{d}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]+\bm{W}Q_{d}(\bm{W})\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}.

In the above display, step (a) follows from the induction hypothesis
\bm{Y}^{d}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\;{Q}_{d}(\bm{W})\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}
. and step (b) uses a standard result on the concentration of quadratic forms of rotationally invariant matrices (see Fact
1
in Appendix
A.1.2
). We define the polynomial
Q_{d+1}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
as:

Q_{d+1}(\lambda)=\theta\cdot\mathbb{E}[Q_{d}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]+\lambda Q_{d}(\lambda)\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R}.

(104)

Hence,
\bm{Y}^{d+1}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\;{Q}_{d+1}(\bm{W})\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}
, as desired. This proves the first claim made in the lemma by induction.

Proof of Claim (2)

The proof of the second claim follows from an analogous induction argument. Specifically, we show by induction that:

\displaystyle\bm{Y}^{d}\cdot\bm{v}\;

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\;\bm{W}^{d}\cdot\bm{v}\quad\forall\;d\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

As before, we assume the claim holds for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
, and we verify it for
d+1
:

\displaystyle\bm{Y}^{d+1}\cdot\bm{v}

\displaystyle=\left(\theta\cdot\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}\bm{x}_{\star}^{\mathsf{T}}}{N}+\bm{W}\right)\cdot\bm{Y}^{d}\cdot\bm{v}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}\left(\theta\cdot\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}\bm{x}_{\star}^{\mathsf{T}}}{N}+\bm{W}\right)\cdot\bm{W}^{d}\cdot\bm{v}

\displaystyle=\theta\cdot\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}\bm{W}^{d}\bm{v}}{N}+\bm{W}^{d+1}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}\bm{W}^{d+1}\cdot\bm{v},

where step (a) follows from the induction hypothesis and step (b) uses the assumption
\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}\bm{W}^{d}\bm{v}/N\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0
. This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

B.3
Proof of Lemma
7

Proof of Lemma
7
.

We prove the second claim, the proof of the first claim is analogous. Since the spectral measure of
\bm{W}
converges to
\mu
(recall Assumption
1
),

\displaystyle\frac{\Tr[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{W})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\bm{W})]}{N}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\mathsf{\Lambda})],\;\;\frac{\Tr[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{W})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\bm{W})]}{N}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\mathsf{\Lambda})].

To obtain a more convenient formula for the limit which does not involve the transformed polynomials
(\widetilde{\Psi}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
, we observe that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{W})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\bm{W})\bm{x}_{\star}}{N}\overset{\text{\tiny{Lem. \ref{lem:aux1}}}}{=}\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{Y}){\Psi}_{t}(\bm{Y})\bm{x}_{\star}}{N}.

where the equality in step (a) follows from standard concentration results for quadratic forms of rotationally invariant matrices (see Fact
1
in Appendix
A.1.2
). Notice that the RHS of the above display can be written as an expectation with respect to
\nu_{N}
, the spectral measure of
\bm{Y}
in the direction of
\bm{x}_{\star}
. Indeed, if
\lambda_{1:N}(\bm{Y})
and
\bm{u}_{1:N}(\bm{Y})
denote the eigenvalues and corresponding eigenvectors of
\bm{Y}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{Y}){\Psi}_{t}(\bm{Y})\bm{x}_{\star}}{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\langle{\bm{x}_{\star}},{\bm{u}_{i}(\bm{Y})}\right\rangle^{2}\cdot\Psi_{s}(\lambda_{i}(\bm{Y})){\Psi}_{t}(\lambda_{i}(\bm{Y}))

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi_{s}(\lambda)\Psi_{t}(\lambda)\;\nu_{N}(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\lambda).

By Lemma
1
(item (1)), we know that
\nu_{N}
converges weakly to
\nu
. Hence,

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\Tr[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{W})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\bm{W})]}{N}=\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{W})\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\bm{W})\bm{x}_{\star}}{N}=\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})\Psi_{t}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})],\quad\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu,

as claimed.
∎

B.4
Proof of Lemma
8

Proof.

Recall that we started with a general OAMP algorithm (cf. Definition
3
) with polynomial matrix denoising functions:

\displaystyle{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t}(\bm{Y})\cdot f_{t}({\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{x}_{t-1};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N},

(105)

with associated state evolution random variables

(\mathsf{X}_{\star},(\mathsf{X}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}};\mathsf{A}),

and constructed an auxiliary OAMP algorithm:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{t}\cdot\widetilde{\Psi}_{t}(\bm{W})\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}+\Psi_{t}(\bm{W})\cdot f_{t}^{\perp}(\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{t-1};\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N},

(106)

which is intended as an easy-to-analyze approximation to the original OAMP algorithm in (
105
). In the above display:

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}f_{t}(\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t-1};\mathsf{A})],\quad f_{t}^{\perp}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{t-1};x_{\star},a)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}f_{t}(x_{1},\dotsc,x_{t-1};a)-\alpha_{t}x_{\star},

(107)

and
(\widetilde{\Psi}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
are the transformed polynomials associated with matrix denoisers
(\Psi_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
, which were constructed in Lemma
6
. Our goal is to show that for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
and any
i\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle(\bm{x}_{\star},\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{t};\bm{a})\overset{W_{2}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}),\quad\frac{\left\langle{\bm{W}^{i}f^{\perp}_{t}(\bm{x}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{x}_{t-1};\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{a})},{\bm{x}_{\star}}\right\rangle}{N}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}0.

We will show that the auxiliary OAMP algorithm can be re-written as an algorithm whose dynamics have characterized in prior work
[
26
, Theorem 2]
.
To this end, we fix a
i\in\mathbb{N}
and a
t\in\mathbb{N}
. Introduce the random variables:

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu,\quad\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu.

For any
s\leq t
, we define:

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{s}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\Big(\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{W})-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\Big)\cdot\bm{x}_{\star},\quad\bm{w}_{s}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\Psi_{s}(\bm{W})\cdot f_{s}^{\perp}(\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{s-1};\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{a}),

(108a)

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{t+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}(\bm{W}^{i}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{\Lambda^{i}}]\cdot\bm{I}_{N})\cdot f_{t}^{\perp}(\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{t-1};\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{a}).

(108b)

Comparing (
106
) and (
108
), we conclude that:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{s}=\beta_{s}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}+\alpha_{s}\cdot\bm{v}_{s}+\bm{w}_{s}\quad\forall\;s\;\leq\;t,

(109)

where we defined:

\displaystyle\beta_{s}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\alpha_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\overset{\text{\tiny{Lem. \ref{lem:nu-trick}}}}{=}\alpha_{s}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})]\quad\forall\;s\leq t.

Hence, (
108
) can be re-expressed as:

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{s}

\displaystyle=\Big(\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\bm{W})-\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\Big)\cdot\bm{x}_{\star},

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{s}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:aux-OAMP-to-prior}}}}{=}\Psi_{s}(\bm{W})\cdot f_{s}^{\perp}(\beta_{1}\bm{x}_{\star}+\alpha_{1}\bm{v}_{1}+\bm{w}_{1},\dotsc,\beta_{s-1}\bm{x}_{\star}+\alpha_{s-1}\bm{v}_{s-1}+\bm{w}_{s-1};\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{a}).

The above algorithm is an instance of a Vector Approximate Message Passing (VAMP) algorithm, as defined in
[
26
, Section 4.1.2]
. Moreover,
[
26
, Theorem 2]
shows that:

\displaystyle(\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{v}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{v}_{t},\bm{w}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{w}_{t+1};\bm{a})\overset{W_{2}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{V}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{V}_{t},\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t+1};\mathsf{A}),

(110)

where the collection of random variables:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A}),\quad(\mathsf{V}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{V}_{t},\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t+1})

are mutually independent. The random variables
(\mathsf{V}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{V}_{t},\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t+1})
have zero mean and are jointly Gaussian. The covariance matrices of
(\mathsf{V}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{V}_{t})
and
(\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t+1})
are given by the recursion:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{s}\mathsf{V}_{\tau}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),\widetilde{\Psi}_{\tau}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\overset{\text{\tiny{Lem. \ref{lem:nu-trick}}}}{=}\mathrm{Cov}[\Psi_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}),\Psi_{\tau}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})]\quad\forall\;s,\tau\leq t,

(111a)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{W}_{s}\mathsf{W}_{\tau}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}[\Psi_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),\Psi_{\tau}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{F}_{s}\mathsf{F}_{t}]-\alpha_{s}\alpha_{t})\quad\forall\;s,\tau\leq t,

(111b)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{s}\mathsf{W}_{\tau}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),\Psi_{\tau}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{F}_{\tau}\mathsf{X}_{\star}]-\alpha_{s})\quad\forall\;s,\tau\leq t.

(111c)

In the above display, for any
\tau\leq t
,

\mathsf{F}_{\tau}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}f_{s}(\beta_{1}\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{1}\mathsf{V}_{1}+\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\beta_{s\tau-1}\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{\tau-1}\mathsf{V}_{\tau-1}+\mathsf{W}_{\tau-1};\mathsf{A}).

Consequently,

\displaystyle(\bm{x}_{\star},\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{t};\bm{a})\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:aux-OAMP-to-prior}}}}{=}(\bm{x}_{\star},\beta_{1}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}+\alpha_{1}\cdot\bm{v}_{1}+\bm{w}_{1},\dotsc,\beta_{t}\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}+\alpha_{t}\cdot\bm{v}_{t}+\bm{w}_{t};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt\overset{W_{2}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\beta_{1}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{1}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{1}+\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\beta_{t}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{t}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{t}+\mathsf{W}_{t};\mathsf{A})\quad\text{[Using \eqref{eq:prior-work-SE}]}.

(112)

We claim that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{\tau}\mathsf{W}_{\tau^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\;\tau^{\prime},\tau\leq t,

(113a)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\beta_{1}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{1}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{1}+\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\beta_{t}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{t}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{t}+\mathsf{W}_{t};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{d}}}{=}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}).

(113b)

The above claims are easily proved by induction. The base case is immediate, and if we assume:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{\tau}\mathsf{W}_{\tau^{\prime}}]=0\quad\forall\;\tau\leq t,\;\tau^{\prime}<s

(114a)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\beta_{1}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{1}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{1}+\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\beta_{s-1}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{s-1}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{s-1}+\mathsf{W}_{s-1};\mathsf{A})\overset{\text{\tiny{d}}}{=}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{s-1};\mathsf{A}),

(114b)

for some
s\leq t
as our induction hypothesis, we can complete the induction step:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{V}_{\tau}\mathsf{W}_{s}]\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:SE-cov-prior-work}}}}{=}\mathrm{Cov}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{\tau}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),\Psi_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{F}_{s}\mathsf{X}_{\star}]-\alpha_{s})\quad\text{ for any $\tau\leq t$}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}[\widetilde{\Psi}_{\tau}(\mathsf{\Lambda}),\Psi_{s}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{F}_{s}(\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{s-1};\mathsf{A})\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}]-\alpha_{s})

[by induction hypothesis (
114
)]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:perp-f-recall}}}}{=}0.

On the other hand, by comparing (
111
) and the description of the joint distribution of the state evolution random variables
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{s};\mathsf{A})
corresponding to the original OAMP algorithm (
105
) provided in Definition
3
we conclude that:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\beta_{1}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{1}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{1}+\mathsf{W}_{1},\dotsc,\beta_{s}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\alpha_{s}\cdot\mathsf{V}_{s}+\mathsf{W}_{s};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{d}}}{=}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{s};\mathsf{A}),

which proves the claims in (
113
) by induction. Combining (
112
) with (
113
) we conclude that:

\displaystyle(\bm{x}_{\star},\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc,\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{t};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\overset{W_{2}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}),

(115)

which proves the first claim made in the lemma. To prove the second claim, we observe that:

\displaystyle\frac{\left\langle{\bm{W}^{i}f^{\perp}_{t}(\bm{x}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{x}_{t-1};\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{a})},{\bm{x}_{\star}}\right\rangle}{N}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:v-w-def}}}}{=}\frac{\left\langle{\bm{w}_{t+1}},{\bm{x}_{\star}}\right\rangle}{N}+\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\frac{\left\langle{f_{t}^{\perp}(\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{1},\dotsc\widetilde{\bm{x}}_{t-1};\bm{x}_{\star},\bm{a})},{\bm{x}_{\star}}\right\rangle}{N}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{W}_{t+1}\mathsf{X}_{\star}]+\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}f_{t}^{\perp}(\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t-1};\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=0.

In the above display, the convergence in the second step follows from (
110
) and (
115
). The equality in the last step follows by recalling that
\mathsf{W}_{t+1}
is a mean-zero Gaussian random variable independent of
\mathsf{X}_{\star}
and the definition of
f_{t}^{\perp}
in (
107
) ensures
\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}f_{t}^{\perp}(\mathsf{X}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{X}_{t-1};\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{A})]
= 0.
∎

Appendix C
State Evolution for Optimal OAMP (Proposition
1
)

Under the assumption
\mathbb{E}\mathrm{Var}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{A}]\in(0,1)
, for the readers convenience, we recall that the optimal OAMP algorithm is given by (cf. (
17
)):

\displaystyle{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t}}}\left(1+\frac{1}{{\rho}_{t}}\right)\cdot\Psi_{\star}(\bm{Y};{\rho}_{t})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}({\bm{x}}_{t-1};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t-1}).

(116)

The estimator returned by the optimal OAMP algorithm at iteration
t
is:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\varphi(\bm{x}_{t};\bm{a}|\omega_{t}).

(117)

In the above equations, the matrix denoiser
\Psi_{\star}
used by the optimal OAMP algorithm is given by:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\star}(\lambda;\rho)

\displaystyle=1-\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]\right)^{-1}\cdot\frac{\phi(\lambda)}{\phi(\lambda)+\rho}\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R},\;\rho\;\in\;(0,\infty),

(118)

where
\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu
and the function
\phi:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
was introduced in Lemma
1
(recall (
5
)). The parameters
{\omega}_{t}
and
{\rho}_{t}
are computed using the following recursion, initialized with
\omega_{0}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}0
:

\displaystyle{\rho}_{t}={F}_{2}(\omega_{t-1}),\;\omega_{t}={F}_{1}(\rho_{t})\quad\text{where}\quad{F}_{2}(\omega)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega})}-1,\quad{F}_{1}(\rho)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}1-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}}\right]}.

(119)

Let
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},(\mathsf{X}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}};\mathsf{A})
denote the state evolution random variables associated with the above algorithm. We will rely on the following intermediate lemma.

Lemma 9
.

We have,

1.

The function
{F}_{1}
is a continuous non-decreasing function which maps
(0,\infty)
to
[0,1)
and satisfies
\lim_{\rho\rightarrow\infty}{F}_{1}(\rho)<1
.

2.

The function
{F}_{2}
is a continuous non-decreasing function which maps
[0,1)
to
(0,\infty)
.

We defer the proof of this lemma to the end of this appendix (Appendix
C.1
), and present the proof of Proposition
1
.

Proof of Proposition
1
.

We prove each of the two claims below.

Proof of Claim (1)

We will show by induction that:

\displaystyle\omega_{t}\in[0,1),\quad\rho_{t}\in(0,\infty),\quad(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A})\text{ form a scalar Gaussian channel with SNR $\omega_{t}$.}

(120)

Assuming this claim, we can compute the asymptotic MSE of the estimator
\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}=\varphi(\bm{x}_{t};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t})
returned by the OAMP algorithm:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{Thm. \ref{thm:SE}}}}{=}\mathbb{E}|\varphi(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t})-\mathsf{X}_{\star}|^{2}\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\omega_{t}).

In the above display, step (a) follows by observing that
\varphi(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t})
is the MMSE estimator for the scalar Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A})
(which has SNR
\omega_{t}
). We now focus on proving (
120
) by induction. We assume that the claim (
120
) holds at step
t
as the induction hypothesis and show that it also holds at step
t+1
. Indeed, since
\omega_{t}\in[0,1)
, Lemma
9
guarantees that
\rho_{t+1}={F}_{2}(\omega_{t})\in(0,\infty)
and
\omega_{t+1}={F}_{1}(\rho_{t+1})\in[0,1)
. Next, we verify that
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{t+1};\mathsf{A})
form a scalar Gaussian channel with SNR
\omega_{t+1}
. From Definition
3
, we know that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{X}_{t+1}|(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\beta_{t+1}\mathsf{X}_{\star},\sigma_{t+1}^{2}\right),

where:

\displaystyle\beta_{t+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t}{})]\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t+1}{}}(1-\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t+1}))}\cdot,

\displaystyle\sigma_{t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\{\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t}{})]\}^{2}\cdot\mathrm{Var}[{\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]}{{\omega}_{t+1}{}(1-\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t+1}))^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\{\mathbb{E}[\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}^{2}(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t}{})]-(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t}{})])^{2}\}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]}{{\omega}_{t+1}{}(1-\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t+1}))^{2}}.

In the above equations
\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu
and
\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu
. We begin by simplifying
\beta_{t+1}
. By the induction hypothesis,
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A})
forms a Gaussian channel with SNR
{\omega}_{t}{}
. A convenient property of the DMMSE estimator for a scalar Gaussian channel is the following identity (see Lemma
2
in Appendix
A.2
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t}{})]=\mathbb{E}[\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}^{2}(\mathsf{X}_{t};\mathsf{A}|{\omega}_{t}{})]=1-\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}{}).

(121)

To compute
\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]
, we consider the Lebesgue decomposition of
\nu
into the absolutely continuous part
\nu_{\parallel}
and the singular part
\nu_{\perp}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:optimal-OAMP-denoiser-2}}}}{=}1-\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]\right)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})+\rho_{t+1}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}1-\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]\right)^{-1}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\phi(\lambda)}{\phi(\lambda)+\rho_{t+1}}\nu_{\parallel}(\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\lambda)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(b)}}}{=}1-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho_{t+1}}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho_{t+1}}}\right]}\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:rho-omega-recall}}}}{=}\omega_{t+1}.

(122)

In the above display, step (a) follows by recalling that
\phi(\lambda)=0
for
\nu_{\perp}
-a.e.
\lambda
(Lemma
1
, item (2)) and step (b) follows from recalling that the density of
\nu_{\parallel}
is given by
\mu(\cdot)/\phi(\cdot)
(Lemma
1
, item (3)).
Plugging in the identities (
121
) and (
122
) into the expression for
\beta_{t+1}
, we obtain:

\beta_{t+1}=\frac{(1-\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}{}))\cdot\omega_{t+1}}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t+1}{}}(1-\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t+1}))}\overset{\text{\tiny{}}}{=}\sqrt{{\omega}_{t+1}{}}.

(123)

Similarly we can also simplify
\sigma_{t+1}^{2}
by exploiting the identities (
121
) and (
122
):

\displaystyle\sigma_{t+1}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:Gauss-channel-convenient}}}}{=}\frac{\mathrm{Var}[{\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]+\tfrac{\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t+1})}{1-\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t+1})}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]}{{\omega}_{t+1}{}}

(124a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:rho-omega-recall}}}}{=}\frac{\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]-(\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}])^{2}+\tfrac{1}{{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]}{{\omega}_{t+1}{}}

(124b)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:convenient-formula-2}}}}{=}\frac{\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]+\tfrac{1}{{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]-\omega_{t+1}^{2}}{{\omega}_{t+1}{}}.

(124c)

After a straightforward (but tedious) calculation analogous to the one used to derive (
122
), we obtain the identity:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]+\tfrac{1}{{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda}\;;{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}{})}]

\displaystyle=\omega_{t+1},

(124d)

which implies that
\sigma_{t+1}^{2}=1-\omega_{t+1}
.

Hence,

\mathsf{X}_{t+1}|(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\sqrt{{\omega}_{t+1}{}}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star},1-{\omega}_{t+1}{}\right),

(125)

which verifies that
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{X}_{t+1};\mathsf{A})
forms a Gaussian channel with SNR
{\omega}_{t+1}{}
.

Proof of Claim (2)

Recall that the sequences
\{\omega_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\rho_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
are generated via the recursion:

\displaystyle{\rho}_{t}

\displaystyle={F}_{2}(\omega_{t-1})\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t-1})}-1,

(126a)

\displaystyle\omega_{t}

\displaystyle={F}_{1}(\rho_{t})\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}1-\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}_{t}}\right]\right)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}_{t}}\right],

(126b)

where
\omega_{0}=0
. We first prove the monotonicity of
\{\omega_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
using an induction argument. Note that
\omega_{t}={F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}(\omega_{t-1})
. First, note that
\omega_{1}\geq\omega_{0}=0
(recall we have already shown
\omega_{t}\geq 0
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
). Since the composite function
{F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}
is non-decreasing (Lemma
9
), it follows that
{F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}(\omega_{1})\geq{F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}(\omega_{0})
, namely,
\omega_{2}\geq\omega_{1}
. Repeating this argument proves that
\{\omega_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is non-decreasing. Moreover,
\{\omega_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded sequence taking values in
[0,1)
(proved in the first claim). Hence,
\{\omega_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to a limit point
\omega_{\star}\in[0,1]
. We eliminate the possibility that
\omega_{\star}=1
by observing that Lemma
9
guarantees that:

\displaystyle\omega_{\star}=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\omega_{t+1}=\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}{F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}(\omega_{t})\leq\lim_{\rho\rightarrow\infty}{F}_{1}(\rho)<1.

Lemma
9
also guarantees the continuity of
{F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}
on
[0,1)
, and hence, the limit point
\omega_{\star}
satisfies the equation
\omega_{\star}={F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}(\omega_{\star})
.
Since the sequence
\{\rho_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is obtained by applying a continuous and non-decreasing function
{F}_{2}
to a non-decreasing and convergent sequence
\{\omega_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
, we conclude that
\{\rho_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is non-decreasing and converges to a limit
\rho_{\star}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}{F}_{2}(\omega_{\star})\in(0,\infty)
. Observe that the limit points satisfy the equations:
\omega_{\star}={F}_{1}\circ{F}_{2}(\omega_{\star}),\;\rho_{\star}={F}_{2}(\omega_{\star})
, which can be expressed as
\omega_{\star}={F}_{1}(\rho_{\star}),\;\rho_{\star}={F}_{2}(\omega_{\star})
as claimed. This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

C.1
Proof of Lemma
9

Proof of Lemma
9
.

We prove the claims made about
{F}_{1}
and
{F}_{2}
below.

Properties of
{F}_{1}

Recall that:

\displaystyle{F}_{1}(\rho)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}1-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}}\right]}\quad\forall\;\rho\;\in\;(0,\infty).

(127)

The continuity of
{F}_{1}(\rho)
follows from the Dominated Convergence Theorem. To verify the monotonicity of
{F}_{1}
, we calculate its derivative:

\begin{split}{F}_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)&=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right)^{2}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{(\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho)^{2}}\right]}{\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]\right)^{2}}\geq 0,\end{split}

where the inequality follows from Chebyshev’s association inequality
[
14
, Theorem 2.14]
, which states that for any two random variables
(X,Z)
such that
Z\geq 0
and any two non-decreasing functions
f,g
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z]\mathbb{E}[f(X)g(X)Z]\geq\mathbb{E}[f(X)Z]\cdot\mathbb{E}[g(X)Z].

Indeed, applying this inequality with
X=\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})
,
f(X)=X
,
g(X)=X+\rho
and
Z=1/(\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho)^{2}
shows that the numerator of
{F}_{1}^{\prime}(\rho)
is non-negative. Finally, we check that
{F}_{1}:(0,\infty)\mapsto[0,1)
. Notice from (
127
) that for any
\rho\in(0,\infty)
,
{F}_{1}(\rho)<1
. On the other hand, since
{F}_{1}(\rho)\geq 0
since, thanks to the monotonicity of
{F}_{1}
we have:

\displaystyle{F}_{1}(\rho)\geq{F}_{1}(0)=1-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}\right]\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}1-\nu_{\parallel}(\mathbb{R})\geq 0.

In the above display, the step (a) follows by recalling from Lemma
1
(item (3)) that the density of
\nu_{\parallel}
, the absolutely continuous part of
\nu
is given by
\mu(\cdot)/\phi(\cdot)
and by observing that
\nu_{\parallel}(\mathbb{R})\leq\nu(\mathbb{R})=1
. Last we compute:

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\rightarrow\infty}{F}_{1}(\rho)

\displaystyle=1-\lim_{\rho\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{\rho}}\right]}=1-\lim_{\rho\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho^{-1}\cdot\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{1}}\right]}{\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\rho^{-1}\cdot\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+{1}}\right]}=1-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]}<1,

as claimed.

Properties of
{F}_{2}

Recall that:

\displaystyle{F}_{2}(\omega)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega})}-1.

The continuity and monotonicity of the DMMSE function
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\cdot)
(see From Lemma
4
in Appendix
A.2
) implies that
{F}_{2}
continuous and non-decreasing. Next, we verify that
{F}_{2}:[0,1)\mapsto(0,\infty)
. Notice that for any
\omega\in[0,1)
,
{F}_{2}(\omega)\geq{F}_{2}(0)=(\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(0))^{-1}-1=(\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0))^{-1}-1>0
(recall that
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)\in(0,1)
was one of the hypothesis assumed in Proposition
1
). Moreover, for any
\omega<1
,
{F}_{2}(\omega)<\infty
since:

\displaystyle\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\omega)\geq\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\omega)\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{>}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(1)=0,

where (a) follows from the fact that
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\cdot)
is strictly decreasing if
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)\in(0,1)
(see Fact
2
in Appendix
A.2
). Hence,
{F}_{2}:[0,1)\mapsto(0,\infty)
. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of the Optimality Result (Theorem
2
)

D.1
Proof of Proposition
3

We consider a given iterative algorithm (Definition
4
) of the form:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t}(\bm{Y})\cdot f_{t}({\bm{r}}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{r}_{t-1};\bm{a})+g_{t}({\bm{r}}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{r}_{t-1};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},

(128)

which returns the following estimator after
t
iterations:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{r}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\psi_{t}({\bm{r}}_{1},\dotsc,{\bm{r}}_{t};\bm{a}),

(129)

Our goal will be to construct a lifted OAMP algorithm (Definition
5
), which can approximate the estimator above. Throughout the proof, we will refer to the given iterative algorithm (
128
) as the
target algorithm
. Our construction of the desired lifted OAMP algorithm proceeds in two steps.

Step 1 (Divergence Removal)

We first implement the target algorithm (
128
) using an
intermediate OAMP algorithm
which takes the form:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}

\displaystyle=\bm{\bar{}}{\Psi}_{S}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}({\bm{v}}_{<t,\bullet};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty.

(130)

In the above display,

1.

The iterates generated by the algorithm are indexed by two indices, a time index
t
, which takes values in
\mathbb{N}
, and a set index
S
, which can be any finite and non-empty subset of
\mathbb{N}
.

2.

For any finite subset
S=\{s_{1},s_{2},\dotsc,s_{k}\}
with sorted elements
s_{1}<s_{2}<\dotsb<s_{k}
, we define the matrix denoiser:

\displaystyle\Psi_{S}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\Psi_{s_{k}}(\lambda)\cdot\Psi_{s_{k-1}}(\lambda)\cdot\dotsb\cdot\Psi_{s_{1}}(\lambda),\quad\bm{\bar{}}{\Psi}_{S}(\lambda)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\Psi_{S}(\lambda)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}\left[\Psi_{S}(\mathsf{\Lambda})\right]\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R}.

3.

The iterates are generated in the following sequence:

(a)

In step (1), we generate the iterate
{\bm{v}}_{1,\{1\}}
.

(b)

In step (2), we generate the new iterates
{\bm{v}}_{1,\{1,2\}}
and
{\bm{v}}_{2,\{1\}},{\bm{v}}_{2,\{1,2\}}
.

(c)

At step
(t)
, we generate the new iterates:

\displaystyle\{{\bm{v}}_{1,S}:S\subset[t],t\in S\},\dotsc,\{{\bm{v}}_{t-1,S}:S\subset[t],t\in S\},\{{\bm{v}}_{t,S}:S\subset[t]\}.

We use the notation
{\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet}
to denote the collection of iterates that have been generated at the end of
t
steps:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet}

\displaystyle=\{{\bm{v}}_{s,S}:s\leq t,\;S\subset[t]\}.

The notation
{\bm{v}}_{<t,\bullet}
denotes the collection of iterates that have been generated before the
t
th step, which is the same as the collection
{\bm{v}}_{\leq(t-1),\bullet}
.

4.

The functions
(F_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
are continuously differentiable, Lipschitz and satisfy the divergence-free requirement:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,S}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}(\mathsf{V}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\;S\subset[t-1],\;s<t,

where
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\{\mathsf{V}_{t,S}:t\in\mathbb{N},S\subset\mathbb{N}\};\mathsf{A})
are the state evolution random variables associated with the intermediate OAMP algorithm in (
130
).

5.

Furthermore, the iterates generated by the intermediate OAMP algorithm in (
130
) can approximate the iterates of the target algorithm in (
128
). Specifically, for each
t\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a postprocessing function
H_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{t\cdot 2^{t}+k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
, which is continuously differentiable and Lipschitz, which satisfies:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{t}-H_{t}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet},\bm{a})\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=0.

(131)

The construction of the intermediate OAMP algorithm with the properties stated above is presented in the proof of the following Lemma
10
, whose proof is deferred to Section
D.1.1
.

Lemma 10
.

For any target algorithm of the form (
128
), there is an intermediate OAMP algorithm of the form (
130
), whose iterates can approximate the iterates generated by the target algorithm in the sense of (
131
).

Step 2: Polynomial Approximation

Next, by approximating the functions
(\Psi_{S})_{S\subset\mathbb{N}}
by polynomials, we construct a lifted OAMP algorithm, whose iterates can approximate the iterates generated by the intermediate OAMP algorithm (
130
). This construction is presented in the following Lemma
5
, whose proof is deferred to Section
D.1.2
.

Lemma 11
.

For each
D\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a degree-
D
lifted AMP algorithm (Definition
5
):

\displaystyle{\bm{w}}_{t,i}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{Y}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\right)\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet}^{(D)},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t-1,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})\quad t\in\mathbb{N},\;i\in[D],

(132)

which can approximate the iterates produced by the intermediate OAMP algorithm (
130
) in the following sense: for any
D\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\in\mathbb{N}
, and any finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{N}
, there is a homogeneous linear postprocessing function
h_{t,S}^{(D)}:\mathbb{R}^{D}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
which has the property that the vector
h^{(D)}_{t,S}({\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet}^{(D)})
approximates the intermediate OAMP iterate
{\bm{v}}_{t,S}
in the sense:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plimsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|h_{t,S}^{(D)}({\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet}^{(D)})-{\bm{v}}_{t,S}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=0

(133)

With these preliminary results, we can now present the proof of Proposition
3
.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Throughout the proof, we shorthand an approximation result like (
131
) using the notation:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}H_{t}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

(134)

Likewise, we shorthand an approximation result like (
133
) using:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}\quad\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty.

(135)

Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
together show that the lifted OAMP algorithm on (
132
) can approximate the iterates of the target algorithm in (
128
):

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}H_{t}({\bm{v}}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{v}}_{t,\bullet};\bm{a})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}}H_{t}(\{h^{(D)}_{s,R}({\bm{w}}_{s,\bullet}^{(D)}):s\leq t,R\subset[t]\};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

(136)

In the above display, the approximation in step (a) follows from (
131
) and the approximation in step (b) follows by replacing each iterate
{\bm{v}}_{s,R},\;s\in[t],R\subset[t]
of the intermediate OAMP algorithm by its approximation in (
133
), and exploiting the fact that
H_{t}
is a Lipschitz function. For convenience, we define the composite postprocessing functions
\{H^{(D)}_{t}:t\in\mathbb{N},D\in\mathbb{N}\}
:

\displaystyle H^{(D)}_{t}(w_{\leq t,\bullet};a)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}H_{t}(\{h^{(D)}_{s,R}({w}_{s,\bullet}):s\leq t,R\subset[t]\};a)\quad\forall\;w_{\leq t,\bullet}\in\mathbb{R}^{tD},\;a\in\mathbb{R}^{k}.

With this definition, (
136
) can be expressed as:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t}\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}H^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{\leq t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

Plugging in the above approximation in (
129
), we have the following approximation for the estimator
\widehat{\bm{r}}_{t}
returned by the target algorithm:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{r}}_{t}\quad\overset{\text{\tiny{$N,D\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\quad\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}\;\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\;\psi_{t}(H^{(D)}_{1}({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a}),H^{(D)}_{2}({\bm{w}}_{\leq 2,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a}),\dotsc,H^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{\leq t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a});\bm{a}).

(137)

Hence, we have constructed an estimator
\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}
which can be computed by running a degree-
D
lifted OAMP algorithm for
t
steps, and approximates the estimator
\widehat{\bm{r}}_{t}
returned by the target iterative algorithm after
t
steps. This proves the claim of Proposition
3
.
∎

We now present the proofs of Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
.

D.1.1
Divergence Removal (Proof of Lemma
10
)

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Consider a target iterative algorithm of the form:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t}(\bm{Y})\cdot f_{t}({\bm{r}}_{1},\dotsc,{\bm{r}}_{t-1};\bm{a})+g_{t}({\bm{r}}_{1},\dotsc,{\bm{r}}_{t-1};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

(138)

Our goal is to construct an intermediate OAMP algorithm of the form:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}

\displaystyle=\bm{\bar{}}{\Psi}_{S}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}({\bm{v}}_{<t,\bullet};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty.

(139)

For each
t\in\mathbb{N}
, we wish to design a postprocessing function
H_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{t\cdot 2^{t}+k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
which allows us to approximate the iterates of the target algorithm with the iterates of the intermediate OAMP algorithm in the sense:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|{\bm{r}}_{t}-H_{t}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet},\bm{a})\|^{2}}{N}\rightarrow 0\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

(140)

We will shorthand approximation statements of the form (
131
) using the notation:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}H_{t}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N}.

(141)

The construction proceeds by induction. Suppose that we have specified the first
t
functions
(\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{s})_{s\leq t}
and the post-processing functions
(H_{s})_{s\leq t}
for
t
iterations. We now extend our construction to the
(t+1)
th iteration. Recall the update rule for
{\bm{r}}_{t+1}
:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t+1}=\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot f_{t+1}({\bm{r}}_{\leq t};\bm{a})+g_{t+1}({\bm{r}}_{\leq t};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\;\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})f_{t+1}(H_{1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq 1,\bullet};\bm{a}),\dotsc,H_{t}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a}))+g_{t+1}(H_{1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq 1,\bullet};\bm{a}),\dotsc,H_{t}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})),

where the approximation in the last step follows from the induction hypothesis
{\bm{r}}_{s}\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\quad H_{s}({\bm{v}}_{\leq s,\bullet};\bm{a})
for any
s\leq t
, along with the fact that
\|\Psi_{t}(\bm{Y})\|_{\op}
is uniformly bounded as
N\rightarrow\infty
and the functions
f_{t+1},g_{t+1}
are Lipschitz. We introduce the composite functions:

\displaystyle{F}_{t+1}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}f_{t+1}(H_{1}(v_{\leq 1,\bullet};a),H_{2}(v_{\leq 2,\bullet};a),\dotsc,H_{t}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};a);a)\quad v_{\leq t,\bullet}\in\mathbb{R}^{t2^{t}},\;a\in\mathbb{R}^{k},

\displaystyle{G}_{t+1}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}g_{t+1}(H_{1}(v_{\leq 1,\bullet};a),H_{2}(v_{\leq 2,\bullet};a),\dotsc,H_{t}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};a);a)\quad v_{\leq t,\bullet}\in\mathbb{R}^{t2^{t}},\;a\in\mathbb{R}^{k}.

With these definitions we have that,

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\quad\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot{F}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a}).

(142)

The above expression suggests that we should use the function
{F}_{t+1}
in the
t+1
step of the intermediate OAMP algorithm. However, since
F_{t+1}
is not divergence-free (cf. Definition
3
), we define:

\displaystyle\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t+1}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};a)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}F_{t}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};a)-\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{R\subset[t]}b_{s,R}\cdot v_{s,R}\quad\forall\;v_{\leq t,\bullet}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\cdot 2^{t}},\;a\in\mathbb{R}^{k}.

In the above display, we introduced the scalars:

\displaystyle{b}_{s,R}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\partial_{s,R}F_{t+1}(\mathsf{V}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\right]\quad\forall\;s\leq t,\;R\subset[t],

where
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\{\mathsf{V}_{s,R}:s\leq t,R\subset[t]\}\;;\mathsf{A})
denote the state evolution random variables corresponding to the iterates of the intermediate OAMP algorithm that have already been constructed (by the induction hypothesis). Notice that by construction,
\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t+1}
satisfies the divergence-free requirement:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,S}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t+1}(\mathsf{V}_{\leq t;\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\;s\leq t,\;S\subset[t].

We can now extend our construction of the intermediate OAMP algorithm to the
(t+1)
th step:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t+1,S}

\displaystyle=\bm{\bar{}}{\Psi}_{S}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;S\subset\mathbb{N}\quad 1\leq|S|<\infty.

(143)

Next, we define the postprocessing function
H_{t+1}
. For convenience, we introduce the vectors
(\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{\tau})_{\tau\in[t+1]}
:

\displaystyle\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{\tau}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{\tau}({\bm{v}}_{<\tau,\bullet};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;\tau\in[t+1].

(144)

With these definitions, we can express the approximation in (
142
) as:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot{F}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})

\displaystyle{\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}}\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{t+1}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}{b}_{s,S}\cdot\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot{\bm{v}}_{s,S}+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a}).

(145)

For any
S\subset[t+1]
we define:

\displaystyle m_{S}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\Psi_{S}(\Lambda)].

With this definition, we can express the approximation in (
145
) as:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t+1}\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{t+1}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot{\bm{v}}_{s,S}+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{t+1}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot(\Psi_{S}(\bm{Y})-{m}_{S}\bm{I}_{N})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{s}+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{t+1}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot(\Psi_{\{t+1\}\cup S}(\bm{Y})-m_{S}\Psi_{t+1}(\bm{Y}))\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{s}+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})

\displaystyle{\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:intermediate-OAMP},\eqref{eq:constructed-LAMP-new-iterate}}}}{=}}{\bm{v}}_{t+1,\{t+1\}}+\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot({\bm{v}}_{s,S\cup\{t+1\}}-m_{S}\cdot{\bm{v}}_{s,\{t+1\}})\right)+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+m_{\{t+1\}}\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{t+1}+\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot(m_{S\cup\{t+1\}}-m_{S}\cdot m_{\{t+1\}})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\bm{F}}_{s}\right).

(146)

Recalling (
144
),

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t+1}\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}{\bm{v}}_{t+1,\{t+1\}}+\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot({\bm{v}}_{s,S\cup\{t+1\}}-m_{S}\cdot{\bm{v}}_{s,\{t+1\}})\right)+{G}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt+m_{\{t+1\}}\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})+\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot(m_{S\cup\{t+1\}}-m_{S}\cdot m_{\{t+1\}})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{s}({\bm{v}}_{\leq s,\bullet};\bm{a})\right).

In light of the above expression, we define the postprocessing function for the
t+1
th iteration as:

\displaystyle H_{t+1}(v_{\leq t+1,\bullet};a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}v_{t+1,\{t+1\}}+\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}\cdot(v_{s,S\cup\{t+1\}}-m_{S}\cdot v_{s,\{t+1\}})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+{G}_{t+1}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};\bm{a})+m_{\{t+1\}}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t+1}(v_{\leq t,\bullet};a)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{S\subset[t]}b_{s,S}(m_{S\cup\{t+1\}}-m_{S}m_{\{t+1\}})\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{s}(v_{\leq s,\bullet};a)\right).

Hence, we can approximate the
t+1
th iterate of the target algorithm using the iterates of the intermediate OAMP algorithm:

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{t+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{$N\rightarrow\infty$}}}{\simeq}H_{t+1}({\bm{v}}_{\leq t+1,\bullet};\bm{a}).

This completes the construction of the intermediate OAMP algorithm with all the desired properties by induction.
∎

D.1.2
Polynomial Approximation (Proof of Lemma
11
)

Proof of Lemma
11
.

Recall the intermediate OAMP algorithm takes the form:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}

\displaystyle=\bm{\bar{}}{\Psi}_{S}(\bm{Y})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}({\bm{v}}_{<t,\bullet};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty.

(147)

To implement this algorithm using a degree-
D
lifted OAMP algorithm, we begin by repeating the arguments used in the proof of Lemma
5
to construct an OAMP algorithm of the form:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}=

\displaystyle\left(\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\bm{I}_{N}\right)\left(\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}({\bm{v}}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{R\subset[t-1]}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,R}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}(\mathsf{V}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]{\bm{v}}_{s,R}^{(D)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty,

(148)

which approximates the iterates generated by the intermediate OAMP algorithm in the sense that,

\displaystyle\limsup_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plimsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}-{\bm{v}}_{t,S}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=0\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty.

(149)

In the above display:

•

The matrix denoiser
\Psi_{S}^{(D)}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is a degree-
D
polynomial which approximates the matrix denoiser
\Psi_{S}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and is constructed using the Weierstrass approximation theorem.

•

(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\{\mathsf{V}^{(D)}_{t,S}:t\in\mathbb{N},S\subset\mathbb{N}\};\mathsf{A})
denote the state evolution random variables associated with (
148
).

We claim that there is a degree-
D
lifted AMP algorithm (Definition
5
):

\displaystyle{\bm{w}}_{t,i}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{Y}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\right)\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;i\in[D],

(150)

along with homogeneous linear postprocessing maps

\displaystyle h^{(D)}_{t,S}:\mathbb{R}^{D}\mapsto\mathbb{R}\quad t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty,

which can exactly recover the iterates generated by the degree-
D
approximation to the intermediate OAMP algorithm (
148
):

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=h^{(D)}_{t,S}({\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;t\in\mathbb{N},\;S\subset\mathbb{N},\;1\leq|S|<\infty.

(151)

Observe that (
149
) and (
151
) immediately imply Lemma
11
. Indeed, for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
and any finite subset
S\subset\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle\limsup_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plimsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|h^{(D)}_{t,S}({\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet}^{(D)})-{\bm{v}}_{t,S}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:low-degree-appx-claim2}}}}{=}\limsup_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plimsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}-{\bm{v}}_{t,S}\|^{2}}{N}\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:low-degree-appx-claim1}}}}{=}0,

as desired. Hence, the remainder of the proof consists of constructing the lifted OAMP algorithm and proving (
151
).

Construction of the lifted OAMP Algorithm

We will construct the desired lifted OAMP algorithm (
150
) by induction. As our induction hypothesis, we will assume that we have specified the functions
\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{1},\dotsc,\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{t-1}
, as well as the homogenous linear postprocessing maps:

\displaystyle h^{(D)}_{s,S}:\mathbb{R}^{D}\mapsto\mathbb{R}\quad s<t,\;S\subset[t-1],\;S\geq 1,

so that the iterates resulting lifted OAMP algorithm
\{{\bm{w}}_{s,i}^{(D)}:s<t,\;i\in[D]\}
can reconstruct the following iterates of the degree-
D
approximation to the intermediate OAMP algorithm:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{s,S}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=h^{(D)}_{s,S}({\bm{w}}_{s,\bullet}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;s<t,\;S\subset[t-1],\;|S|>1.

(152)

We now extend our construction to step
t
. To do so, we need to construct the function
\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{t}
used in the
t
th iteration of the lifted OAMP algorithm, as well as the linear postprocessing maps:

\displaystyle h^{(D)}_{t,S}:\mathbb{R}^{D}\mapsto\mathbb{R}\quad S\subset[t],\;S\geq 1,

\displaystyle h^{(D)}_{s,S}:\mathbb{R}^{D}\mapsto\mathbb{R}\quad S\subset[t],S\ni t,

which ensure that:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=h^{(D)}_{t,S}({\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;S\subset[t],\;|S|>1,

(153)

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{s,S}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=h^{(D)}_{s,S}({\bm{w}}_{s,\bullet}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;s<t,\;S\subset[t],\;S\ni t.

(154)

We will present the proof for (
153
), the proof for (
154
) is analogous. We begin by recalling the update rule for
{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}
:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}=

\displaystyle\left(\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\bm{I}_{N}\right)\underbrace{\left(\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}({\bm{v}}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{R\subset[t-1]}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,R}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}(\mathsf{V}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]{\bm{v}}_{s,R}^{(D)}\right)}_{(\diamondsuit)}.

(155)

Using the induction hypothesis in (
152
), we can express the vector
(\diamondsuit)
in the above display as:

\displaystyle(\diamondsuit)

\displaystyle=\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}(\{h^{(D)}_{s,R}({\bm{w}}_{s,\bullet}^{(D)})\};\bm{a})-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{R\subset[t-1]}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,R}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}(\mathsf{V}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]h^{(D)}_{s,R}({\bm{w}}_{s,\bullet}^{(D)}).

In light of the above expression, we define
F_{t,D}
as the composite function (for any
w_{<t,\bullet}\in\mathbb{R}^{(t-1)D},\;a\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
)

\displaystyle F^{(D)}_{t}(w_{<t,\bullet};a)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}(\{h^{(D)}_{s,R}(w_{s,\bullet}):s<t,R\subset[t-1],|R|>1\};a),

(156)

and the divergence-free iterate denoiser:

\displaystyle\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{t}(w_{<t,\bullet};a)

\displaystyle=F^{(D)}_{t}(w_{<t,\bullet};a)-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,i}F^{(D)}_{t}(\mathsf{W}^{(D)}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]\cdot w_{s,i},

(157)

where
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\{\mathsf{W}_{s,i}:s<t,i\in[D]\};\mathsf{A})
are the state evolution random variables associated with the lifted OAMP algorithm (
150
) that have been constructed so far (as a part of the induction hypothesis). We now extend our construction of the desired lifted OAMP algorithm to step
t
. At step
t
, the lifted OAMP algorithm generates the iterates:

\displaystyle{\bm{w}}_{t,i}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{Y}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\right)\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})\quad i\in[D].

(158)

Introducing the shorthand notation
\overline{\Psi}_{S}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}
for convenience, we can express (
155
) as:

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:dup-F}}}}{=}\;\overline{\Psi}_{S}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})\left(F^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{R\subset[t-1]}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,R}\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}(\mathsf{V}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\mathsf{A})]h^{(D)}_{s,R}({\bm{w}}_{s,\bullet}^{(D)})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(b)}}}{=}\overline{\Psi}_{S}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})\left(F^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})-\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\mathbb{E}[\partial_{s,i}F^{(D)}_{t}(\mathsf{W}^{(D)}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]{\bm{w}}_{s,i}^{(D)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:dup-F-df}}}}{=}\left(\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\bm{Y})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\bm{I}_{N}\right)\bm{\bar{}}{F}^{(D)}_{t}({\bm{w}}_{<t,\bullet}^{(D)};\bm{a})

(159)

where the step marked (b) follows by relating the derivatives of the function
F^{(D)}_{t}
and the function
\bm{\bar{}}{F}_{t}
using the chain rule and by exploiting the linearity of postprocessing maps
\{h_{s,R}\}
. Recall that
\Psi^{(D)}_{S}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is a degree-
D
polynomial, and so, it can be expressed as a linear combination of the monomials
\{\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{i}:i\in[D]\}
:

\displaystyle\Psi^{(D)}_{S}(\lambda)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\Psi_{S}^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\chi^{(D)}_{S}[i]\cdot(\lambda^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}])\quad i\in[D].

Using this representation, the formula for
{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}
obtained in (
159
) can be expressed as a linear combination of the newly constructed lifted OAMP iterates in (
158
):

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\chi^{(D)}_{S}[i]\cdot{\bm{w}}_{t,i}^{(D)}.

Hence, we define the postprocessing function
h^{(D)}_{t,S}
as:

\displaystyle h^{(D)}_{t,S}(w_{t,\bullet})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\chi^{(D)}_{S}[i]\cdot w_{t,i}\quad\forall\;w_{t,\bullet}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}.

which is linear and ensures that
{\bm{v}}_{t,S}^{(D)}=h^{(D)}_{t,S}({\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet}^{(D)})
, as desired. This completes the construction of the desired lifted OAMP algorithm by induction and concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

D.2
Proof Proposition
4

Consider any degree-
D
lifted OAMP algorithm (Definition
5
) of the form:

\displaystyle{\bm{w}}_{t,i}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{Y}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda\sim\nu}}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\right)\cdot f_{t}\big({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t-1,\bullet};\bm{a}\big)\quad i\in[D],\;t\in\mathbb{N},

(160)

which returns the following estimator after
t
iterations:

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}}=h_{t}({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet};\bm{a}).

Let
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
denote the state evolution random variables associated with the first
t
iterations of the lifted OAMP algorithm, which form a Gaussian channel in the sense of Definition
2
. We observe that, by Corollary
1
:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\bm{x}_{\star}-{\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-h(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{\geq}\min_{h\in L^{2}(\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})}\mathbb{E}[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-h(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}).

(161)

In the above display, the inequality in step (a) follows by observing that since the function
h
is a Lipschitz function (as required by Definition
5
), and hence,
h\in L^{2}(\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. Furthermore, thanks to Assumption
2
holds, the lower bound above is tight since we can choose
h
as the MMSE estimator for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
.

In light of (
161
), for a
t
iteration lifted OAMP algorithm which uses functions
f_{1:t}
in its iterations, we define:

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}),

where
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
are the state evolution random variables associated with the lifted OAMP algorithm. Notice that the RHS of the above display depends on the functions
f_{1:t}
implicitly, since the distribution of the state evolution random variables is determined by
f_{1:t}
(recall Definition
5
).

A convenient formula for
{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})

To determine the optimal lifted OAMP algorithm, we need to optimize the functional
{M}_{t}
with respect to
f_{1:t}
. To do so, we first derive a convenient formula for
{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})=\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. Lemma
3
in Appendix
A.2
shows that the MMSE (and other relevant properties) of a multivariate Gaussian channel, such as
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
can be expressed in terms of the MMSE of an
effective scalar Gaussian channel
:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{S}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\left\langle{v_{\mathrm{opt}}},{\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet}}\right\rangle;\mathsf{A}),

(162)

whose observation random variable
\mathsf{S}
is a linear combination of the observation random variables of the original multivariate Gaussian channel. The weights of linear combination are given by
v_{\mathrm{opt}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
(which we will sometimes shorthand as
v_{\mathrm{opt}}
, when the multivariate Gaussian channel in question is clear from the context). The effective scalar Gaussian channel operates at SNR
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
, and the MMSE of the original multivariate channel and the effective scalar channel are connected via the formula:

\displaystyle\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})=\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{S};\mathsf{A})=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}\left(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\right),

where for
\omega\in[0,1]
,
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\omega)
is the MMSE of a scalar Gaussian channel at SNR
\omega
(recall Definition
2
). The following lemma provides a formula for the effective SNR
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. To state this formula, recall from Definition
5
that the joint distribution of the state evolution random variables
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
is given by:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\sim\pi,

(163a)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet})|(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\mathsf{X_{\star}}\cdot\alpha\otimes q,\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma\right).

In the above display, the entries of
q\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
,
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, and
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
are given by:

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}],

(163b)

\displaystyle Q_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}]\right)\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{j}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{j}]\right)\right],\;\Gamma_{ij}=\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i},\mathsf{\Lambda}^{j}].

(163c)

the entries of
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
and
\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times t}
are given by:

\displaystyle\alpha_{s}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot{f}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})],\quad\Sigma_{s\tau}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[{f}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot{f}_{\tau}(\mathsf{W}_{<\tau,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]\quad\forall\;s,\tau\;\in\;[t],

(163d)

and
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker (or tensor) product for matrices.

Lemma 12
.

Consider a lifted OAMP algorithm which uses functions
f_{1},f_{2},\dotsc,f_{t}
with
f_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{(i-1)\cdot D+k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
in the first
t
iterations. Let
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
be the state evolution random variables associated with the algorithm. Then,

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}q^{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{T}}\left[Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right]^{\dagger}q&:\kappa_{t}\in[0,1)\\
0&:\kappa_{t}=1.\end{cases}

Moreover, if
\kappa_{t}<1
, then,

\displaystyle v_{\mathrm{opt}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})}}\cdot(\Sigma^{\dagger}\alpha)\otimes[(1-\kappa_{t})Q+\kappa_{t}\Gamma]^{\dagger}q,

where the scalar
\kappa_{t}\in[0,1]
is defined as
4
4

4

For a collection of functions
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}
,
\mathrm{Span}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})
denotes the set of all functions that can be written as a linear combination of
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}
.
:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t})\bigg\}.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is presented in Section
D.2.1
.
∎

Combining the effective SNR formula from the lemma above with (
162
), we obtain the following expression for
{M}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})
:

\displaystyle{M}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

(164a)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}))={m}(\kappa_{t}),

(164b)

where we introduced the function
{m}:[0,1]\mapsto[0,1]
defined as follows:

\displaystyle{m}(\kappa)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\begin{cases}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}\left(q^{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{T}}\left[Q+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\cdot\Gamma\right]^{\dagger}q\right)&:\kappa\in[0,1)\\
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)&:\kappa=1,\end{cases}

(164c)

where
q,Q,\Gamma
are as defined in (
163
).

Monotonicity of
{m}(\cdot)

The following monotonicity property of the function
{m}
introduced in (
164
) will play an important role in the proof of Proposition
4
.

Lemma 13
.

The function
{m}:[0,1]\mapsto[0,1]
is non-decreasing.

Proof.

See Section
D.2.2
.
∎

A Greedy Approach Minimizing
{M}_{t}

To derive the optimal OAMP algorithm, our goal is to solve the variational problem:

\displaystyle\min_{f_{1}}\min_{f_{2}}\dotsb\min_{f_{t}}{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}),

(165)

Recall that Proposition
4
claims that the iterate denoisers
(f_{t}^{\star})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
that optimize (
165
) are defined recursively: once the optimal denoisers
f^{\star}_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}^{\star}
have been specified, the optimal denoiser for the
t+1
iteration is the DMMSE estimator for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
, where
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
denote the state evolution random variables corresponding to the first
t
iterations. We will show that these choices for the iterate denoisers correspond to a
greedy approach
to solving the optimization problem in (
165
):

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f_{1}}{M}_{1}(f_{1}),\ f_{2}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f_{2}}{M}_{2}(f_{1}^{\star},f_{2}),\ \dotsc,\quad f_{t}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f_{t}}{M}_{t}(f_{1:t-1}^{\star},f_{t}).

Proving this requires us to characterize the solution of the simpler (compared to (
165
)) variational problem:

\displaystyle\min_{f_{t}}{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}),

which is done in the following lemma.

Lemma 14
.

Let:

1.

f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1}
with
f_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{(i-1)\times D+k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
be a fixed collection of iterate denoisers which satisfy the requirements imposed in the definition of lifted OAMP algorithms (Definition
5
) ;

2.

(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1;\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
be the state evolution random variables associated with the first
t-1
iterations of a lifted OAMP algorithm which uses the functions
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1}
in the first
t-1
iterations;

3.

f_{t}^{\sharp}
denote the DMMSE estimator for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1;\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
.

Then, for any iterate denoiser
