f_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{(t-1)\times D+k}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
which satisfies the requirements imposed in Definition
5
, we have:

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})\geq{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}^{\sharp})={m}(\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}),

(166)

where
\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}({M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1})))
.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is presented in Section
D.2.3
.
∎

Using the above intermediate lemmas, we can now present the proof of Proposition
4
.

Proof of Proposition
4
.

To prove the claim of Proposition
4
, we need to show that for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
and for any choice of iterate denoisers
f_{1:t}
which satisfy the requirements imposed in Definition
5
,

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})\geq{M}_{t}(f_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,f_{t}^{\star}),

(167)

where
f_{1:t}^{\star}
denote the functions used in the iterations of the optimal lifted OAMP algorithm, which are specified recursively as follows: after the functions
f^{\star}_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1}^{\star}
have been specified,
f_{t}^{\star}
was defined as the DMMSE estimator for the Gaussian channel corresponding to the first
t-1
iterations of the resulting lifted OAMP algorithm. By the Lemma
14
,
f_{1:t}^{\star}
are precisely the functions derived from the greedy heuristic:

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f_{1}}{M}_{1}(f_{1}),\ f_{2}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f_{2}}{M}_{2}(f_{1}^{\star},f_{2}),\ \dotsc,\quad f_{t}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f_{t}}{M}_{t}(f_{1:t-1}^{\star},f_{t}),

where the minimization is understood to be over iterate denoisers which satisfy the requirements imposed in Definition
5
. We will obtain the desired conclusion in (
167
) by induction. As our induction hypothesis, we assume that for any choice of the iterate denoisers
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1}
, we have:

\displaystyle{M}_{t-1}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1})\geq{M}_{t-1}(f_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,f_{t-1}^{\star}).

(168)

In order to obtain the desired conclusion (
167
), we observe that:

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{\geq}{m}(\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}),\text{ where }\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}({M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1})))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(b)}}}{\geq}{m}(\kappa_{t}^{\star}),\text{ where }\kappa_{t}^{\star}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}({M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1}^{\star})))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(c)}}}{=}{M}_{t}(f_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,f_{t}^{\star}).

In the above display:

•

Step (a) used the lower bound for
{M}_{t}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t})
provided in Lemma
14
.

•

In step (b), we observed by the induction hypothesis that
{M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1}^{\star})\leq{M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1})
. Since
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}
and
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}
are non-increasing functions (Lemma
2
and Lemma
4
), we conclude that
\kappa_{t}^{\star}\leq\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}
. Finally, since
{m}
is non-increasing (Lemma
13
), we concluded that
{m}(\kappa_{t}^{\sharp})\geq{m}(\kappa_{t}^{\star})
.

•

In step (c), we again appealed to Lemma
14
:

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,f_{t}^{\star})

\displaystyle=\min_{f_{t}}{M}_{t}(f_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,f_{t})

\displaystyle={m}(\kappa_{t}^{\star}),\text{ where }\kappa_{t}^{\star}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}({M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1}^{\star}))).

This concludes the proof of Proposition
4
.
∎

D.2.1
Proof of Lemma
12

Proof of Lemma
12
.

Recall from Definition
5
that the joint distribution of the state evolution random variables is given by:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\sim\pi,

(169)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet})|(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\mathsf{X_{\star}}\cdot\alpha\otimes q,\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma\right).

(170)

In the above display, the entries of
q\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
,
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, and
\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
are given by:

\displaystyle q_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}],

(171a)

\displaystyle Q_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}]\right)\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{j}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{j}]\right)\right],\;\Gamma_{ij}=\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i},\mathsf{\Lambda}^{j}].

(171b)

and the entries of
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
and
\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times t}
are given by:

\displaystyle\alpha_{s}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot{f}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})],\quad\Sigma_{s\tau}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[{f}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot{f}_{\tau}(\mathsf{W}_{<\tau,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]\quad\forall\;s,\tau\;\in\;[t].

(172)

The proof of the claim is obtained by applying the effective SNR formula for general multivariate Gaussian channels stated as Lemma
3
in Appendix
A.2
to the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{W}_{\leq t;\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. According to this formula:

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})=\begin{cases}\frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}&\text{ if }\alpha\otimes q\in\mathrm{Range}(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma),\\
1&\text{ if }\alpha\otimes q\notin\mathrm{Range}(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma),\end{cases}

(173a)

where:

\displaystyle\vartheta\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\alpha\otimes q^{\top}(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma)^{\dagger}\alpha\otimes q.

(173b)

The claim of the lemma is obtained by simplifying the above formula.

Orthogonalization

To obtain the simplified formula stated in the lemma, it will be convenient to construct an orthonormal basis for
\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1:t})
. Let
r=\mathrm{Rank}(\Sigma)
and
\widehat{f}_{1},\widehat{f}_{2},\dotsc,\widehat{f}_{r}:\mathbb{R}^{(t-1)D}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
denote the basis functions constructed by orthogonalizing the functions
{f}_{1:t}
with respect to the state evolution random variables
(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
, via the Gram-Schmidt procedure. These basis functions are orthonormal in the sense that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{f}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\widehat{f}_{\tau}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&:s=\tau\\
0&:\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We can express the functions
{f}_{1:t}
as a linear combination of the basis functions:

\displaystyle{f}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{r}L_{\tau s}\widehat{f}_{s}\quad\forall\;\tau\;\in\;[t],\quad L_{\tau s}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[{f}_{\tau}(\mathsf{W}_{<\tau,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\widehat{f}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]\quad\forall\;s\ \in[r],\;\tau\in[t].

(174)

Let
L
denote the
t\times r
matrix whose entries are given by the coefficients
(L_{\tau s})_{\tau\in[t],s\in[r]}
defined above. Using the decomposition in (
174
), along with the definitions of
\alpha,\Sigma
in (
172
), we obtain the following alternative expressions for
\alpha
and
\Sigma
:

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle=LL^{\top},\quad\alpha=L\widehat{\alpha},\quad\widehat{\alpha}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}(\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\widehat{f}_{1}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})],\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\widehat{f}_{2}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]\cdots,\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\widehat{f}_{t}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})])^{\top}.

(175)

Similarly, by exploiting the fact that
\widehat{f}_{1:t}
form an orthonormal basis for
\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1:t})
, we have the following alternate formula for
\kappa_{t}
:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t})\bigg\}

\displaystyle=1-\|\widehat{\alpha}\|^{2}\in[0,1].

(176)

These alternate expressions for
\alpha,\Sigma,\kappa_{t}
will be useful in our subsequent calculations.

Useful Linear Algebraic Results

The proof relies on the following intermediate linear algebraic claims.

\displaystyle\text{For any }\lambda\geq 0,\quad q\in\mathrm{Range}(Q+\lambda\Gamma),

(177)

\displaystyle\text{If }\kappa_{t}<1,

(178)

\displaystyle q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q\neq 1\Leftrightarrow\alpha\otimes q\in\mathrm{Range}(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma).

We defer the justification of these claims to the end of the proof.

Corner Cases

We begin by verifying the claim of the lemma for two corner cases. Consider the situation where
\kappa_{t}=1
. In this case, (
175
) and (
176
) imply that
\alpha=0
. As a result, using the formula for the effective SNR for a general multivariate Gaussian channel given in Lemma
3
(Appendix
A.2
), the effective SNR of the Gaussian channel in (
169
) is
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}=0
, as claimed. Next, we consider the case when:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}<1,

\displaystyle\quad q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q=1.

From (
178
), we conclude that:

\displaystyle\alpha\otimes q\notin\mathrm{Range}(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma).

Hence, from (
173
), we conclude that the effective SNR of the Gaussian channel in (
169
) is
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}=1
, as claimed.

Derivation of the claimed formulae

The remaining task is to prove the claim of the lemma when:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}<1,

\displaystyle\quad q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q\neq 1,

and we work under the above assumption for the remainder of the proof. From (
178
), we conclude that:

\displaystyle\alpha\otimes q\in\mathrm{Range}(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma).

From (
173
), we have that:

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta},

(179)

\displaystyle v_{\mathrm{opt}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\vartheta+\vartheta^{2}}}\cdot(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma)^{\dagger}\cdot\alpha\otimes q

(180)

where:

\displaystyle\vartheta\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\alpha\otimes q^{\top}(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma)^{\dagger}\alpha\otimes q.

(181)

To compute the pseudoinverse
(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma)^{\dagger}
, we define
\overline{\alpha}
as the unit vector along
\widehat{\alpha}
and
P
as the projector orthogonal to
\widehat{\alpha}
:

\displaystyle\overline{\alpha}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{\widehat{\alpha}}{\|\widehat{\alpha}\|},\quad P\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}I-\overline{\alpha}\overline{\alpha}^{\top}.

(182)

We have,

\displaystyle(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:alpha-Sigma-alt}}}}{=}(L^{\dagger}\otimes I)^{\top}\cdot[\widehat{\alpha}\widehat{\alpha}^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top}-\Gamma)+I\otimes\Gamma]^{\dagger}\cdot(L^{\dagger}\otimes I)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:kappa-alt},\eqref{eq:alpha-bar-P}}}}{=}(L^{\dagger}\otimes I)^{\top}\cdot[\overline{\alpha}\overline{\alpha}^{\top}\otimes\{(1-\kappa_{t})\cdot(Q-qq^{\top})+\kappa_{t}\cdot\Gamma\}+P\otimes\Gamma]^{\dagger}\cdot(L^{\dagger}\otimes I)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=(L^{\dagger}\otimes I)^{\top}\cdot[\overline{\alpha}\overline{\alpha}^{\top}\otimes\{(1-\kappa_{t})\cdot(Q-qq^{\top})+\kappa_{t}\cdot\Gamma\}^{\dagger}+P\otimes\Gamma^{\dagger}]\cdot(L^{\dagger}\otimes I).

The above formula for the pseudo-inverse yields:

\displaystyle(\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma)^{\dagger}\cdot\alpha\otimes q

(183)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=(L^{\dagger\top}\widehat{\alpha})\otimes[\{(1-\kappa_{t})\cdot(Q-qq^{\top})+\kappa_{t}\cdot\Gamma\}^{\dagger}\cdot q]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}(1-\kappa_{t})^{-1}\cdot\left\{1-q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q\right\}^{-1}\cdot(L^{\dagger\top}\widehat{\alpha})\otimes\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:alpha-Sigma-alt}}}}{=}(1-\kappa_{t})^{-1}\cdot\left\{1-q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q\right\}^{-1}\cdot(\Sigma^{\dagger}{\alpha})\otimes\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q,

(184)

where we used the Sherman-Morrison formula in (a). Combining the computation above with the definition of
\vartheta
in (
181
) yields the following formula for
\vartheta
:

\displaystyle\vartheta

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:kappa-alt}}}}{=}(\alpha^{\top}\Sigma^{\dagger}\alpha)\cdot(1-\kappa_{t})^{-1}\cdot\left\{1-q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q\right\}^{-1}\cdot q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}\left\{1-q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q\right\}^{-1}\cdot q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q

where the equality marked (a) follows by observing that
\alpha^{\top}\Sigma^{\dagger}\alpha=\|\widehat{\alpha}\|^{2}=1-\kappa_{t}
. Plugging the expression of the previous display in (
179
) yields:

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q,

\displaystyle v_{\mathrm{opt}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})}}\cdot(\Sigma^{\dagger}{\alpha})\otimes\left((1-\kappa_{t})\cdot Q+{\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q

as claimed. To finish the proof, we need to prove the linear algebraic claims in (
177
) and (
178
).

Proof of claim (
177
)

Fix any
\lambda\geq 0
. To show that
q\in\mathrm{Range}(Q+\lambda\Gamma)
, it is sufficient to verify that
q
is orthogonal to
\mathrm{Null}(Q+\lambda\Gamma)
. Towards this goal, we consider any
v\in\mathrm{Null}(Q+\lambda\Gamma)
and aim to show that
\left\langle{v},{q}\right\rangle=0
. Since
v
lies in the null space of
Q+\lambda\Gamma
,

\displaystyle v^{\top}(Q+\lambda\Gamma)v

\displaystyle=0\implies v^{\top}Qv=0,

where the implication is obtained by observing that
Q\succeq 0
and
\Gamma\succeq 0
(cf. (
171
)). Recalling the definition of
q
from (
171
), we obtain:

\displaystyle|\left\langle{q},{v}\right\rangle|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{D}v_{i}\cdot(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}^{i}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}])\bigg]\right|^{2}\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{\leq}\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{D}v_{i}\cdot(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}^{i}-\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}])\bigg|^{2}\right]\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:q-Q-Gamma-def}}}}{=}v^{\top}Qv=0.

In the above display,
\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu,\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu
and the inequality in (a) follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. Hence,
\left\langle{q},{v}\right\rangle=0
for any
v\in\mathrm{Null}(Q+\lambda\Gamma)
, which implies the claim in (
177
).

Proof of claim (
178
)

For convenience, we introduce the definitions:

\displaystyle S\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\alpha\alpha^{\top}\otimes(Q-qq^{\top})+(\Sigma-\alpha\alpha^{\top})\otimes\Gamma,\quad T\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}Q+\frac{\kappa_{t}}{1-\kappa_{t}}\cdot\Gamma.

Assuming
\kappa_{t}<1
, our goal is to show the equivalence:

\displaystyle q^{\top}T^{\dagger}q\neq 1\Leftrightarrow\alpha\otimes q\in\mathrm{Range}(S).

Consider the vector:

\displaystyle v\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}(L^{\dagger\top}\widehat{\alpha})\otimes(T^{\dagger}q).

A short computation reveals that:

\displaystyle Sv

\displaystyle=(1-\kappa_{t})\cdot(1-q^{\top}T^{\dagger}q)\cdot\alpha\otimes q,\quad\left\langle{q},{v}\right\rangle=(1-\kappa_{t})\cdot q^{\top}T^{\dagger}q.

If
q^{\top}T^{\dagger}q\neq 1
, we have found a vector
u=(1-\kappa_{t})^{-1}\cdot(1-q^{\top}T^{\dagger}q)^{-1}\cdot v
such that
Su=\alpha\otimes q
, which certifies that
\alpha\otimes q\in\mathrm{Range}(S)
. On the other hand, if
q^{\top}T^{\dagger}q=1
, then
Sv=0
and we have found a vector
v\in\mathrm{Null}(S)
such that
\left\langle{v},{q}\right\rangle=(1-\kappa_{t})\neq 0
, which certifies that
\alpha\otimes q\notin\mathrm{Range}(S)
. This concludes the proof of this lemma.
∎

D.2.2
Proof of Lemma
13

Proof of Lemma
13
.

Recall from (
164
) that:

\displaystyle{m}(\kappa)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\begin{cases}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}\left(q^{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{T}}\left[Q+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\cdot\Gamma\right]^{\dagger}q\right)&:\kappa\in[0,1)\\
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)&:\kappa=1.\end{cases}

(185)

The expressions for the vector
q\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
and the
positive semi-definite
matrices
Q,\Gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
appear in (
163
), but will not be needed in the proof. To show that
{m}
is a non-decreasing function, it suffices to verify that for any
0\leq\kappa\leq\kappa^{\prime}<1
, we have
{m}(\kappa)\leq{m}(\kappa^{\prime})\leq{m}(1)
. Indeed we have,

\displaystyle 0\leq\kappa\leq\kappa^{\prime}<1\implies 0\leq\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\leq\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{1-\kappa^{\prime}}\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{\implies}0\preceq Q+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\cdot\Gamma\preceq Q+\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{1-\kappa^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma

(186)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(b)}}}{\implies}q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q\geq q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{1-\kappa^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}\geq 0

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(c)}}}{\implies}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}\left(q^{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{T}}\left[Q+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\cdot\Gamma\right]^{\dagger}q\right)\leq\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}\left(q^{\mkern-1.5mu\mathsf{T}}\left[Q+\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{1-\kappa^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma\right]^{\dagger}q\right)\leq\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0),

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(d)}}}{\implies}{m}(\kappa)\leq{m}(\kappa^{\prime})\leq{m}(1).

In the above display, step (a) follows by observing that
Q,\Gamma
are positive semidefinite (
\preceq
denotes the standard Loewner partial ordering of symmetric matrices defined by the positive semidefinite cone), step (c) relies on the monotonicity of
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\cdot)
(Fact
2
). Finally, step (b) follows from the Fenchel conjugate formula for convex quadratic forms (recall from (
177
) that
q\in\mathrm{Range}(Q+\lambda\Gamma)
for any
\lambda\geq 0
):

\displaystyle q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q=\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{D}}2\left\langle{q},{\xi}\right\rangle-\xi^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa}{1-\kappa}\cdot\Gamma\right)\xi

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:Q-Gamma-psd-order}}}}{\geq}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{D}}2\left\langle{q},{\xi}\right\rangle-\xi^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{1-\kappa^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma\right)\xi=q^{\top}\left(Q+\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{1-\kappa^{\prime}}\cdot\Gamma\right)^{\dagger}q.

This verifies that
{m}
is non-decreasing.
∎

D.2.3
Proof of Lemma
14

Proof of Lemma
14
.

Given a collection of iterate denoisers
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}
, which satisfy the requirements of Definition
5
, our goal is to show that:

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1},f_{2},\dotsc,f_{t-1},f_{t})

\displaystyle\geq{m}(\kappa_{t}^{\sharp})\text{ and,}

(187)

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1},f_{2},\dotsc,f_{t-1},f_{t}^{\sharp})

\displaystyle={m}(\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}),

(188)

where
\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}({M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1})))
and
f_{t}^{\sharp}
is the DMMSE estimator for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
generated by the state evolution random variables associated with the lifted OAMP algorithm which uses the denoisers
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1}
in the first
t-1
iterations. From Lemma
12
, we know that:

\displaystyle{M}_{t}(f_{1},f_{2},\dotsc,f_{t})

\displaystyle={m}(\kappa_{t})

(189a)

where the scalar
\kappa_{t}\in[0,1]
was defined as:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t})\bigg\}.

(189b)

We consider the two claims of the lemma (
187
) and (
188
) one by one.

Proof of Claim (
187
)

In light of the monotonicity of
{m}
(Lemma
13
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t})\bigg\}\geq\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}

(190)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}({M}_{t-1}(f_{1:t-1}))).

(191)

We can lower bound
\kappa_{t}
as follows:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\min_{g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t})}\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{\geq}\min_{g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t})}\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]\text{ s.t. }\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}_{s,i}g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]=0\;\forall\;s\in[t-1],\;i\in[D]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathrm{DMMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}).

(192)

In the above display, the random variables
\mathsf{Z}_{<t,\bullet}
represent the Gaussian noise random variables for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. To obtain the inequality in step (a), we used the fact that any
g\in\mathrm{Span}(f_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t})
satisfies the divergence-free constraints:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}_{s,i}\cdot g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]=0\;\forall\;s\in[t-1],\;i\in[D].

Indeed, by Gaussian integration by parts, for any
i<t
,
s<t
and
j\in[D]
,

\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}_{s,j}\cdot{f}_{i}(\mathsf{W}_{<i;\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

can be expressed as a linear combination of the expected derivatives

\{\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\tau,\ell}{f}_{i}(\mathsf{W}_{<i;\bullet};\mathsf{A})]:\tau<i,\ell\in[D]\},

which are all identically zero thanks to the divergence-free requirement imposed on the iterate denoisers
f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1}
in Definition
5
. We now cast the lower bound on
\kappa_{t}
obtained in (
192
) in the desired form (
190
). Using the DMMSE formula for general multivariate Gaussian channels (stated as Lemma
3
in Appendix Appendix
A.2
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}\geq\mathrm{DMMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}),

where
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}
is the effective SNR of the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. On the other hand, the MMSE formula for general multivariate Gaussian channels (stated as Lemma
3
in Appendix
A.2
) yields:

\displaystyle{M}_{t-1}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1})\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}).

Since
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\cdot)
is a strictly decreasing function (Fact
2
), it has a well-defined inverse
5
5

5

Strictly speaking, Fact
2
guarantees that
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}:[0,1]\mapsto[0,1]
is strictly increasing only when
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{A}]]>0
. In the corner case when
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{A}]]=0
, notice that
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{A}]=0
. Since
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}
and
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}
are non-negative, non-increasing functions (cf. Fact
2
and Lemma
4
), this means that
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(\omega)=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\omega)=0
for any
\omega\in[0,1]
. In this situation, we can define
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}
arbitrarily. Irrespective of our convention,
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}(\omega)=0
, since
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}
is a constant function which takes the value
0
.

\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}
, which can be used to relate the previous two displays:

\displaystyle\kappa_{t}\geq\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}})=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}\circ\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}^{-1}({M}_{t-1}(f_{1},\dotsc,f_{t-1}))\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\kappa_{t}^{\sharp},

(193)

which proves the claim made in (
190
) and concludes the proof of the first part of the lemma.

Proof of Claim (
188
)

In light of the formula in (
189
), it suffices to show that:

\displaystyle\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t-1},{f}_{t}^{\sharp})\bigg\}=\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}.

From (
193
), we already know that:

\displaystyle\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t-1},{f}_{t}^{\sharp})\bigg\}\geq\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}

Hence, we only need to show the upper bound:

\displaystyle\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t-1},{f}_{t}^{\sharp})\bigg\}\leq\kappa_{t}^{\sharp}.

(194)

We upper bound the LHS in (
194
) by taking
g=f_{t}^{\sharp}
and use the DMMSE formula for a general multivariate Gaussian channel (see Lemma
3
in Appendix
A.2
) to obtain:

\displaystyle\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1},\dotsc,{f}_{t-1},{f}_{t}^{\sharp})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[(\mathsf{X}_{\star}-f_{t}^{\sharp}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}))^{2}]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}\mathrm{DMMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{Lem. \ref{lem:scalarization-mmse}}}}{=}\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}})\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:kappa_t_star-DMMSE-relation}}}}{=}\kappa_{t}^{\sharp},

where step (a) follows by recalling that
f_{t}^{\sharp}
was the DMMSE estimator for
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

D.3
Proof of Proposition
5

We begin by reminding the reader that the optimal degree-
D
lifted OAMP algorithm was given by:

\displaystyle{\bm{w}}_{t,i}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{Y}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\right)\cdot f^{\star}_{t}\big({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t-1,\bullet};\bm{a}\big)\quad\forall\;i\in[D],\;t\in\mathbb{N},

(195)

At the end of
t
iterations, the optimal lifted OAMP algorithm estimates
\bm{x}_{\star}
by:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=h_{t}^{\star}({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet};\bm{a}).

Description of the iterate denoisers and post-processing functions

The iterate denoisers functions
f^{\star}_{1},f^{\star}_{2},\dotsc
and the post-processing functions
h_{1}^{\star},h_{2}^{\star},\dotsb
were defined recursively: once
f^{\star}_{1},\dotsc,f_{t}^{\star}
have been specified, let
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
denote the state evolution random variables corresponding to the first
t
iterations of the resulting lifted OAMP algorithm. Then, the iterate denoiser for step
t+1
is the DMMSE estimator for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. Lemma
3
in Appendix
A.2
provides a general formula for the DMMSE estimator of a multivariate Gaussian channel. An application of this formula yields:

\displaystyle f_{t+1}^{\star}(w;a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}\bigg(\left\langle{{v}_{t}^{(D)}},{w}\right\rangle;a\;\big|\;{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\bigg)\quad\forall\;w\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{tD},\;a\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{k}

(196a)

where:

\displaystyle{{v}_{t}^{(D)}}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}v_{\mathrm{opt}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}),\quad{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

(196b)

denote the optimal linear combination and effective SNR of the channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
(as defined in Lemma
3
).
Moreover, the estimator at the end of
t
iterations is obtained by applying the MMSE estimator for the channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
to the iterates:

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{w}}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=h_{t}^{\star}({\bm{w}}_{\leq t,\bullet};a),

(196c)

\displaystyle h_{t}^{\star}(w;a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\varphi\bigg(\left\langle{{v}_{t}^{(D)}},{w}\right\rangle;a\;\big|\;{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\bigg)\quad\forall\;w\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{tD},\;a\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{k}

(196d)

Dynamics of the Optimal Lifted OAMP Algorithm

By Corollary
1
, the joint distribution of
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
is given by:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})\sim\pi,

(197a)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet})|(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59596pt\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\mathsf{X_{\star}}\cdot\alpha_{t}\otimes q^{(D)},\alpha_{t}\alpha_{t}^{\top}\otimes(Q^{(D)}-q^{(D)}{q^{(D)}}^{\top})+(\Sigma_{t}-\alpha_{t}\alpha^{\top}_{t})\otimes\Gamma^{(D)}\right).

In the above display, the entries of
q^{(D)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
,
Q^{(D)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, and
\Gamma^{(D)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
are given by:

\displaystyle q^{(D)}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}]-\mathbb{E}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}],\;Q^{(D)}_{ij}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}]\right)\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{j}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{j}]\right)\right],

(197b)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(D)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i},\mathsf{\Lambda}^{j}].

the entries of
\alpha_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
and
\Sigma_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{t\times t}
are given by:

\displaystyle(\alpha_{t})_{s}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot{f}^{\star}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})],\quad(\Sigma_{t})_{s\tau}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[{f}^{\star}_{s}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot{f}^{\star}_{\tau}(\mathsf{W}_{<\tau,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]\quad\forall\;s,\tau\;\in\;[t],

(197c)

and
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker (or tensor) product for matrices. Finally, the asymptotic MSE of the estimator
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}
returned after
t
iterations is given by:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathsf{X}_{\star}-h_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\right)^{2}\right]=\mathrm{MMSE}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\overset{\text{\tiny{Lem. \ref{lem:scalarization-mmse}}}}{=}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)}).

(197d)

The following lemma provides a formula for the effective SNR
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1:t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
and the optimal linear combination
{{v}_{t}^{(D)}}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}v_{\mathrm{opt}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
at step
t
.

Lemma 15
.

Let
{\omega}_{0}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}0
and
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{A}]]
.

1.

If
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}<1
, then
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}
and
{v}_{t}^{(D)}
admit the following recursive characterization:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t-1}^{(D)}),

\displaystyle{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle={q^{(D)}}^{\top}\left[Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)},

\displaystyle{v}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle={({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\cdot(1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)})^{-1}\cdot e_{t}\otimes\left[Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)},

where
e_{t}=(0,0,\dotsc,1)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{t}
denotes the last standard basis vector. Moreover, for each
t\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}>0,\quad{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}<1\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

2.

If
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}=1
, then
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}=0
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
D.3.1
.
∎

Eliminating Corner Cases

Next, we observe that the proof of Proposition
5
is quite simple in the corner cases
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{A}]]=1
and
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Var}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{A}]]=0
.

Case 1:
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=1
.

In this case, combining (
197d
) and Lemma
15
(Claim 2), we conclude that for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=1.

On the other hand, the optimal OAMP algorithm (recall (
17
) from Section
3.2
) returns the estimator
\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}=\bm{0}
for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}=\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}^{2}]=1=\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N},

as claimed.

Case 2:
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=0
.

In this situation, we observe that for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle 0\leq\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})\overset{\text{\tiny{Fact~\ref{fact:mmse-monotonic}}}}{\leq}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=0\implies\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}=0.

On the other hand, the simplified OAMP algorithm returns the estimator
\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}=\varphi(\bm{a}|0)
for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\mathsf{X}_{\star}-\varphi(\mathsf{A}|0))^{2}]=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=0=\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N},

as claimed.

Hence, in the remainder of the proof, we will assume that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(0)\in(0,1).

(198)

Simplifying the Optimal Lifted OAMP Algorithm

Recall the estimator computed at the end of
t
iterations of the optimal lifted OAMP algorithm is:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}

\displaystyle=h_{t}^{\star}({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t,\bullet}),

where
h_{t}^{\star}
is the MMSE estimator for the channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet})
:

\displaystyle h_{t}^{\star}(w;a)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\varphi\bigg(\left\langle{{v}_{t}^{(D)}},{w}\right\rangle;a|{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\bigg)\quad\forall\;w\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{tD},\;a\;\in\;\mathbb{R}^{k}.

To compute this estimator, we only need to track the following vector
\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}
, which is a linear combination of the lifted OAMP iterates
{\bm{w}}_{\leq t,\bullet}
with weights given by
{v}_{t}^{(D)}
:

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\sum_{i=1}^{D}({v}_{t}^{(D)})_{s,i}\cdot{\bm{w}}_{s,i}.

(199)

Indeed, the estimator returned by the optimal lifted OAMP algorithm can be computed using
\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}
:

\displaystyle{\widehat{\bm{w}}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\varphi(\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}).

In fact,
\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}
can be tracked using a simpler iterative algorithm. To see this, we observe using the formula for
{v}_{t}^{(D)}
from Lemma
15
(Claim 1) that we have:

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{D}({v}_{t}^{(D)})_{t,i}\cdot{\bm{w}}_{t,i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)})\sqrt{{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}}}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left\{\left(Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right)^{\dagger}q^{(D)}\right\}_{i}\cdot{\bm{w}}_{t,i}

Recall that the update equation for
{\bm{w}}_{t,i}
:

\displaystyle{\bm{w}}_{t,i}

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{Y}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}]\cdot\bm{I}_{N}\right)\cdot f^{\star}_{t}\big({\bm{w}}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t-1,\bullet};\bm{a}\big)\quad\forall\;i\in[D],\;t\in\mathbb{N},

In light of the above equations, we introduce the degree-
D
polynomial matrix denoising function:

\displaystyle{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\lambda;\rho)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left\{\left(Q^{(D)}+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right)^{\dagger}q^{(D)}\right\}_{i}\cdot(\lambda^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}])\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R},\;\rho\in(0,\infty],

(200)

as well as the sequence
({\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}-1,\quad{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t-1}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

(201)

This leads to the following formula for
\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}
:

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}}}\left(\frac{1}{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}}+1\right)\cdot{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\bm{Y};{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)})\cdot f_{t}^{\star}({\bm{w}}_{1;\bullet},\dotsc,{\bm{w}}_{t-1;\bullet};\bm{a})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:optimal-LOAMP-denoisers},\eqref{eq:suff-stat-LOAMP}}}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}}}\left(\frac{1}{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}}+1\right)\cdot{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\bm{Y};{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}({\bm{x}}_{t-1}^{(D)};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t-1}^{(D)})

In summary, we have obtained an OAMP algorithm (in the sense of Definition
3
):

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}}}\left(\frac{1}{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}}+1\right)\cdot{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\bm{Y};{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}({\bm{x}}_{t-1}^{(D)};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t-1}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N},

(202)

which can reconstruct the estimator
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}
computed by the optimal degree-
D
lifted OAMP algorithm in (
195
):

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\varphi(\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

Large-degree limit

Notice the similarity between the simplified version of the optimal degree-
D
lifted OAMP algorithm in (
202
) and the optimal OAMP algorithm introduced in (
17
). Our goal will be to show that the performance of the simplified OAMP algorithm in (
202
) converges to the performance of the optimal OAMP algorithm in (
17
) as
D\rightarrow\infty
. To do so, we will rely on the following lemma which shows that the matrix denoiser
{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}
used in the simplified OAMP algorithm (
202
) can be viewed as the minimizer of a variational problem over degree-
D
polynomials. By studying the same variational problem over
L^{2}(\mu+\nu)
, we obtain a candidate for the optimal matrix denoiser in the large degree limit, which turns out to be precisely the matrix denoiser (
17e
) used by the optimal OAMP algorithm in (
17
).

Lemma 16
.

Let
{L}_{D}
and
{L}_{\infty}
denote the following functions on the domain
(0,\infty)
:

\displaystyle{L}_{D}(\rho)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\inf_{\Psi\in\mathcal{P}_{D}}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\quad\text{ subject to }\quad\mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0,

(203)

\displaystyle{L}_{\infty}(\rho)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\inf_{\Psi\in L^{2}(\mu+\nu)}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\quad\text{ subject to }\quad\mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0,

(204)

where
\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu,\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu
,
\mathcal{P}_{D}
denotes the set of all polynomial functions on
\mathbb{R}
with degree at most
D
, and
L^{2}(\mu+\nu)
denotes the set of all real valued functions on
\mathbb{R}
which are square integrable with respect to
\mu+\nu
. Then, we have:

1.

For any
\rho\in(0,\infty)
, the function
{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\cdot;\rho)
defined in (
200
) is the minimizer of the variational problem in (
203
) and,

\displaystyle{L}_{D}(\rho)

\displaystyle=1-q^{\top}_{D}\cdot\left(Q^{(D)}+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right)^{\dagger}\cdot q^{(D)}=1-\mathbb{E}[{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu};\rho)].

2.

For any
\rho\in(0,\infty)
, the function:

\displaystyle\Psi_{\star}(\lambda;\rho)

\displaystyle=1-\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]\right)^{-1}\cdot\frac{\phi(\lambda)}{\phi(\lambda)+\rho}\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R},\;\rho\;\in\;(0,\infty),

is a minimizer of the variational problem in (
204
). In the above display, for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
,
\phi(\lambda)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}{(1-\pi\theta{H}_{\mu}(\lambda))^{2}+\pi^{2}\theta^{2}\mu^{2}(\lambda)}
, where
{H}_{\mu}
is the Hilbert transform of
\mu
and
\theta
is the SNR of the spiked matrix model
\bm{Y}=(\theta/N)\cdot\bm{x}_{\star}\bm{x}_{\star}^{\top}+\bm{W}
.

3.

For any
D\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the function
{L}_{D}
maps the interval
(0,\infty)
to
(0,1]
.

4.

Let
(\rho_{D})_{D\in\mathbb{N}}
be any non-decreasing sequence in
(0,\infty)
which converges to
\rho\in(0,\infty)
as
D\rightarrow\infty
. Then,
{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})\downarrow{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
as
D\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover,
{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho_{D})
converges to
\Psi_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho)
in
L^{2}(\mu+\nu)
as
D\rightarrow\infty
.

5.

For any
\rho\in(0,\infty)
, the function
\Psi_{\star}(\cdot;\rho)
satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu};\rho)]=1-{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is provided in Appendix
D.3.2
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5

We now have all the ingredients to complete the proof of Proposition
5
. Recall that the optimal estimator that can be computed using
t
iterations of a degree-
D
lifted OAMP algorithm is:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\varphi({\bm{x}}_{t}^{(D)};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}),

where the iterates
{\bm{x}}_{t}
are computed using the update rule:

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}}}\left(\frac{1}{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}}+1\right)\cdot{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\bm{Y};{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}({\bm{x}}_{t-1}^{(D)};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t-1}^{(D)})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

(205a)

Furthermore, the limiting mean-squared error of the estimator
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}
is given by:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)}).

(205b)

In the above equations, the parameters
\{{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}},\{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}},\{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
are updated according to the following recursion from Lemma
15
(also recall the definition of
{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}
from (
201
)) initialized with
{\omega}_{0}^{(D)}=0
:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t-1}^{(D)}),\ {\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}=\frac{1}{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}-1,

(205c)

\displaystyle{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=(q^{(D)})^{\top}\left[Q^{(D)}+\frac{1}{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}}\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}1-{L}_{D}({\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}),

where the equality marked (a) follows from Lemma
16
(Claim (2)). To prove Proposition
5
, we need to show that in the large degree limit (
D\rightarrow\infty
), the performance of the estimator
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}
is identical to the performance of the estimator
\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\varphi({\bm{x}}_{t};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t}{})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N},

returned by the optimal OAMP algorithm from (
17
):

\displaystyle{\bm{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\omega}_{t}}}\left(1+\frac{1}{{\rho}_{t}}\right)\cdot\Psi_{\star}(\bm{Y};{\rho}_{t})\cdot\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}({\bm{x}}_{t-1};\bm{a}|{\omega}_{t-1})\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

(206)

In the above equation, the parameters
\{{\omega}_{t}{}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}},\{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}},\{{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}}{}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
are updated according to the following recursion initialized with
{\omega}_{0}=0
:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t}=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t-1}),\quad{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}}=\frac{1}{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}}-1,\quad{\omega}_{t}{}

\displaystyle=1-{L}_{\infty}({{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}}{}).

(207)

Let
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},(\mathsf{X}_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{N}};\mathsf{A})
denote the state evolution random variables corresponding to the above algorithm. We will show by induction that for each
t\in\mathbb{N}
,

1.

for any
D\in\mathbb{N}
, the sequences
the sequences
\{{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}},\{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}},\{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}\}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
generated by (
205c
) satisfy:

\displaystyle{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\in[0,1),\quad{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}\in(0,1),\quad{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}\in(0,\infty)\quad\forall\;t\;\in\;\mathbb{N}.

(208)

In particular, the update equation for
{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}
in (
205c
) is well-defined (avoids division by zero).

2.

{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\uparrow{\omega}_{t}{}\in[0,1),\;{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}\downarrow{\mathsf{d}}_{t}\in(0,1)
, and
{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}^{(D)}\uparrow{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t}}{}\in(0,\infty)
as
D\rightarrow\infty
.

These claims immediately imply Proposition
5
:

\displaystyle\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{x}}_{t}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{Prop. \ref{prop:optimal-OAMP-SE}}}}{=}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}{})\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{=}\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:MMSE-optimal-LOAMP}}}}{=}\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\plim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{t}^{(D)}-\bm{x}_{\star}\|^{2}}{N},

as claimed. In the above display step (a) follows from the claim
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\uparrow{\omega}_{t}{}
as
D\rightarrow\infty
.
We now prove the three claims made above. As the induction hypothesis, we assume the claims hold for some
t\in\mathbb{N}
and verify that they continue to hold at step
t+1
.

Proof of Claim (1)

We focus on proving (
208
) holds at iteration
t+1
, assuming it holds at iteration
t
. The induction hypothesis
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\geq 0
and the monotonicity of
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\cdot)
(see Lemma
4
in Appendix
A.2
) imply that
{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})\leq\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(0)=\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(0)<1
(recall (
198
)). On the other hand, since
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}<1
, by the strict monotonicity of
\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}
(see Fact
2
in Appendix
A.2
), we conclude that
{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})\geq\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})>\mathsf{mmse}_{\pi}(1)=0
. Hence,
{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}\in(0,1)
. Since
{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}^{(D)}=({\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)})^{-1}-1
, we conclude that
{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}^{(D)}\in(0,\infty)
. Finally, since
{\omega}_{t+1}=1-{L}_{D}({\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}^{(D)})
and Lemma
16
guarantees that
{L}_{D}:(0,\infty)\mapsto(0,1]
, we conclude that
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\in[0,1)
, as claimed in (
208
). This proves the first claim.

Proof of Claim (2)

We recall from (
205c
) that
{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})
. By the induction hypothesis
{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\uparrow{\omega}_{t}{}\in[0,1)
. Since
\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(\cdot)
is a non-increasing continuous function (see Lemma
4
in Appendix
A.2
), we conclude that
{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}\downarrow\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}{})\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:snr-dmmse-recursion-lim}}}}{=}{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}{}\in(0,1)
and
{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:snr-dmmse-recursion-degree}}}}{=}({\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)})^{-1}-1\uparrow({\mathsf{d}}_{t+1})^{-1}-1\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:snr-dmmse-recursion-lim}}}}{=}{{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}}\in(0,\infty)
. Finally, recalling the update rule for
{\omega}_{t+1}^{(D)}
from (
205c
) and using Lemma
16
(Claim 4), we conclude that:
{\omega}_{t+1}^{(D)}=1-{L}_{D}({\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}^{(D)})\uparrow 1-{L}_{\infty}({{\mathsf{\rho}}_{t+1}})\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:snr-dmmse-recursion-lim}}}}{=}{\omega}_{t+1}\in[0,1)
. This proves the second claim.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5
.

D.3.1
Proof of Lemma
15

The proof of Lemma
15
relies on the following intermediate result.

Claim 3
.

For any
s,t\in\mathbb{N}
with
s\leq t
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{s-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{s-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})].

We will first prove Lemma
15
assuming this claim. The proof of Claim
3
is provided at the end of this section.

Proof of Lemma
15
.

Recall that for each
t\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle{{v}_{t}^{(D)}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}v_{\mathrm{opt}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}),\quad{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}(\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A}),

where
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},(\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet})_{t\in\mathbb{N}};\mathsf{A})
are the state evolution random variables associated with the optimal lifted OAMP algorithm. In addition, we will find it helpful to introduce the quantity:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,{f}^{\star}_{t})\bigg\}.

Since
\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1}^{\star})\subset\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1}^{\star},{f}^{\star}_{2})\subset\dotsb\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,{f}^{\star}_{t})\subset\dotsb
,
({\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is a non-increasing sequence.

Case 1:
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}<1

Notice that since
({\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)})_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is a non-increasing sequence and
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}<1
, we conclude that
{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}<1
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, which is one of the claims pertaining to this case made in the lemma. To prove the other claims, we will show by induction on
t
that:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t-1}^{(D)}),

(209a)

\displaystyle{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=q^{\top}_{D}\left[Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)},

(209b)

\displaystyle{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle>0,

(209c)

\displaystyle{v}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle={({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\cdot(1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)})^{-1}\cdot e_{1}\otimes\left[\cdot Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)}.

(209d)

As our induction hypothesis we assume that (
209
) holds, and show that (
209
) also holds for
t+1
. Indeed, since
f_{t+1}^{\star}
is the DMMSE estimator for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
, by Lemma
14
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\min\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\left[\{\mathsf{X}_{\star}-g(\mathsf{W}_{\leq t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\}^{2}\right]:g\in\mathrm{Span}({f}_{1}^{\star},\dotsc,{f}^{\star}_{t+1})\bigg\}=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)}).

By appealing to Lemma
12
, we conclude that:

\displaystyle{\omega}_{t+1}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=q^{\top}_{D}\left[Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{}}}{>}0

\displaystyle{v}_{t+1}^{(D)}

\displaystyle={({\omega}_{t+1}^{(D)})^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\cdot(1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)})^{-1}\cdot(\Sigma_{t+1}^{\dagger}\alpha_{t+1})\otimes\left[Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)}.

From Claim
3
, we know that:

\displaystyle(\alpha_{t+1})_{s}

\displaystyle=(\Sigma_{t+1})_{s,t+1}\quad\forall\;s\in\;[t+1]\implies\alpha_{t+1}=\Sigma_{t+1}e_{t+1}\implies\Sigma_{t+1}^{\dagger}\alpha_{t+1}=e_{t+1}.

Hence,

\displaystyle{v}_{t+1}^{(D)}

\displaystyle={({\omega}_{t+1}^{(D)})^{-\frac{1}{2}}}\cdot(1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)})^{-1}\cdot e_{t+1}\otimes\left[Q^{(D)}+\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}}{1-{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right]^{\dagger}q^{(D)},

as desired. This proves the claim of the lemma when
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}<1
.

Case 2:
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}=1

In this situation, we will show by induction that for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=1,\quad{\omega}_{t}^{(D)}=0.

We assume that the above claim holds at step
t
as the induction hypothesis. As in the previous case, by Lemma
14
, we have:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{d}}_{t+1}^{(D)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}({\omega}_{t}^{(D)})=\mathsf{dmmse}_{\pi}(0)={\mathsf{d}}_{0}^{(D)}=1.

Appealing to Lemma
12
, we conclude that
{\omega}_{t+1}^{(D)}=0
, as desired.This proves the claim of the lemma.
∎
We now present the proof of Claim
3
.

Proof of Claim
3
.

By the Tower property, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{s-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet},\mathsf{A}]f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{s-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})].

(210)

Since
\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet},\mathsf{A}]
is the MMSE estimator for the channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. Using the formula for the MMSE estimator for a general multivariate Gaussian channel (see Lemma
3
in Appendix
A.2
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet},\mathsf{A}]

\displaystyle=\varphi\left(\left\langle{\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet}},{v_{\mathrm{opt}}}\right\rangle;\mathsf{A}|\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}\right),

(211)

where we use the shorthand notations
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}
and
v_{\mathrm{opt}}
denote the effective SNR and optimal linear combination
6
6

6

We suppress the dependence of these quantities on the iteration number
t
and the degree
D
of the lifted OAMP for notational convenience since it does not play a role in the proof.
for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t-1,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. Similarly, Lemma
3
gives the following formula for
f_{t}^{\star}
, the DMMSE estimator for this channel:

\displaystyle f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}\left(\left\langle{\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet}},{v_{\mathrm{opt}}}\right\rangle;\mathsf{A}|\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}\right).

(212)

Recall the formula for
\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}
(the DMMSE estimator in a scalar Gaussian channel) provided in Definition
2
(cf. (
11
)):

\displaystyle\bm{\bar{}}{\varphi}(x;a|\omega)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\begin{cases}\left(1-\sqrt{\omega}\cdot\beta(\omega)\right)^{-1}\cdot\left(\varphi(x;a|\omega)-\beta(\omega)\cdot x\right)&:\omega<1\\
\varphi(x;a|\omega)&:\omega=1\end{cases},

(213)

where:

\displaystyle\beta(\omega)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\omega}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}\varphi(\sqrt{\omega}\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\sqrt{1-\omega}\mathsf{Z};\mathsf{A}|\omega)]\quad\text{where }(\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A})\sim\pi,\;\mathsf{Z}|\mathsf{X}_{\star};\mathsf{A}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,1\right).

Combining (
211
), (
212
), and (
213
) yields the following formula relating the MMSE and DMMSE estimators for the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}|\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet},\mathsf{A}]

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\left(1-\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\beta\left(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}\right)\right)\cdot f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})+\beta\left(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}\right)\cdot\left\langle{v_{\mathrm{opt}}},{\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet}}\right\rangle&:\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}<1\\
f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})&:\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}=1.\end{cases}

We substitute the above expression in (
210
). If
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}=1
, the claim of the lemma is immediate. If
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}<1
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\beta\left(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}\right)\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}[f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle+\beta\left(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}\right)\cdot\mathbb{E}[\left\langle{v_{\mathrm{opt}}},{\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet}}\right\rangle\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})].

Let
\mathsf{Z}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{Z}_{t-1,\bullet}
denote the Gaussian noise random variables in the Gaussian channel
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{W}_{1,\bullet},\dotsc,\mathsf{W}_{t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})
. Since
(\mathsf{X}_{\star},\left\langle{v_{\mathrm{opt}}},{\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet}}\right\rangle;\mathsf{A})
forms a scalar Gaussian channel with SNR
\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}
:

\displaystyle\left\langle{v_{\mathrm{opt}}},{\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\sqrt{1-\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}},\quad\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\frac{\left\langle{v_{\mathrm{opt}}},{\mathsf{Z}_{<t,\bullet}}\right\rangle}{\sqrt{1-\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\beta(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}))\cdot\mathbb{E}[f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle+\beta(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}})\cdot\mathbb{E}[(\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\mathsf{X}_{\star}+\sqrt{1-\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}})\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\beta(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}))\cdot\mathbb{E}[f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\omega_{\mathrm{eff}}}\cdot\beta(\omega_{\mathrm{eff}})\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})].

The final equality in the previous display follows by observing that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}}\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}}|\mathsf{X}_{\star},\mathsf{Z}_{<s,\bullet},\mathsf{A}]\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}}|\mathsf{Z}_{<s,\bullet}]\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]=0.

This is because
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}}|\mathsf{Z}_{<s,\bullet}]]
is a linear combination of
\mathsf{Z}_{<s,\bullet}
(since
(\mathsf{Z}_{<s,\bullet},\mathsf{Z}_{\mathrm{eff}})
are jointly Gaussian) and the DMMSE estimator
f_{s}^{\star}
is uncorrelated with the channel noise
\mathsf{Z}_{<s,\bullet}
. Rearranging (
D.3.1
) gives us:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{X}_{\star}\cdot f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[f_{s}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<s,\bullet};\mathsf{A})\cdot f_{t}^{\star}(\mathsf{W}_{<t,\bullet};\mathsf{A})],

which is precisely the claim set out to prove.
∎

D.3.2
Proof of Lemma
16

Proof of Lemma
16
.

We consider each of the claims made in the lemma.

Proof of Claim (1)

To solve the variational problem:

\displaystyle{L}_{D}(\rho)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\inf_{\Psi\in\mathcal{P}_{D}}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\quad\text{ subject to }\quad\mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0,

we parameterize any polynomial
f
of degree at most
D
which satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0
as:

\displaystyle\Psi(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{D}v_{i}\cdot(\lambda^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i})])\quad\forall\;\lambda\in\mathbb{R}.

Hence,

\displaystyle{L}_{D}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\min_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{D}}\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{D}v_{i}\cdot(\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}_{\nu}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i})])-1\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{D}v_{i}\cdot(\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i})])\right|^{2}.

(214)

Expanding the square norms and recalling that the entries of
q^{(D)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
,
Q^{(D)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, and
\Gamma^{(D)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
are given by:

\displaystyle q^{(D)}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}]-\mathbb{E}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}],

(215)

\displaystyle Q^{(D)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{i}-\mathbb{E}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{i}]\right)\cdot\left({\mathsf{\Lambda}}_{\nu}^{j}-\mathbb{E}[{\mathsf{\Lambda}}^{j}]\right)\right],

(216)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(D)}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i},\mathsf{\Lambda}^{j}]\quad\text{ where }\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu,\;\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu,

(217)

we obtain the following formula for
{L}_{D}(\rho)
:

\displaystyle{L}_{D}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\min_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{D}}1+v^{\top}Q^{(D)}v-2\left\langle{q^{(D)}},{v}\right\rangle+\frac{v^{\top}\Gamma^{(D)}v}{\rho}.

The optimizer of the above quadratic form is:

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\left(Q^{(D)}+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right)^{\dagger}\cdot q^{(D)}.

Hence, a minimizer of (
214
) is:

\displaystyle\Psi(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left\{\left(Q^{(D)}+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\Gamma^{(D)}\right)^{\dagger}\cdot q^{(D)}\right\}_{i}\cdot(\lambda^{i}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}[\mathsf{\Lambda}^{i}])\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:matdnsr-D}}}}{=}{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\lambda;\rho)\quad\forall\;\lambda\in\mathbb{R},

and:

\displaystyle{L}_{D}(\rho)

\displaystyle=1-{q^{(D)}}^{\top}\left(Q^{(D)}+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\Gamma_{D}\right)^{\dagger}q^{(D)}=1-\left\langle{q^{(D)}},{v}\right\rangle=1-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}[{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu};\rho)],

as claimed.

Proof of Claim (2)

Our goal is to solve the optimization problem:

\displaystyle{L}_{\infty}(\rho)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\inf_{\Psi\in L^{2}(\mu+\nu)}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\quad\text{ subject to }\quad\mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0.

(218)

This is exactly the problem considered in (
35c
), where we showed that the minimizer of (
218
) is:

\displaystyle\Psi(\lambda)

\displaystyle=1-\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}\left[\frac{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})}{\phi(\mathsf{\Lambda})+\rho}\right]\right)^{-1}\cdot\frac{\phi(\lambda)}{\phi(\lambda)+\rho}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\Psi_{\star}(\lambda;\rho)\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R},

(219)

as claimed.

Proof of Claim (3)

We need to show that for any
D\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the function
{L}_{D}
maps the interval
(0,\infty)
to
(0,1]
. We will show this claim for
{L}_{\infty}
and the exact same argument works for
{L}_{D}
for any
D\in\mathbb{N}
. The definition of
{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
(cf. (
218
)) implies that
{L}_{\infty}(\rho)\geq 0
. Taking
\Psi=0
in (
218
) shows that
{L}_{\infty}(\rho)\leq 1
. To prove Claim (3), we need to verify that if
\rho\in(0,\infty)
then,
{L}_{\infty}(\rho)>0
. We prove this by contradiction. If
{L}_{\infty}(\rho)=0
, then the function
\Psi_{\star}(\cdot;\rho)
, which minimizes the objective in (
218
) satisfies
\mathbb{E}[|\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu};\rho)-1|^{2}]+\rho^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}[|\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho)|^{2}]=0
. This means that:

\displaystyle\nu(\{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}:\Psi_{\star}(\lambda;\rho)=1\})=1,\quad\mu(\{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}:\Psi_{\star}(\lambda;\rho)=0\})=1.

In particular
\mu,\nu
are mutually singular. However, this contradicts Lemma
1
which tells us that
\nu_{\parallel}
, the absolutely continuous part of
\nu
with respect to the Lebesgue measure has density
\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\parallel}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\lambda}(\lambda)=\frac{\mu(\lambda)}{\phi(\lambda)}
, and hence
\nu_{\parallel}\ll\mu
.

Proof of Claim (4)

Consider a non-decreasing sequence
(\rho_{D})_{D\in\mathbb{N}}
which converges to
\rho\in(0,\infty)
as
D\rightarrow\infty
. We begin by observing that
{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})
is a non-increasing sequence:

\displaystyle{L}_{D+1}(\rho_{D+1})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:scrL-D-recall}}}}{=}\inf_{\Psi\in\mathcal{P}_{D+1}}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho_{D+1}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\ \text{ subject to }\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(a)}}}{\leq}\inf_{\Psi\in\mathcal{P}_{D+1}}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho_{D}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\ \text{ subject to }\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{(b)}}}{\leq}\inf_{\Psi\in\mathcal{P}_{D}}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho_{D}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\ \text{ subject to }\ \mathbb{E}[\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda})]=0\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:scrL-D-recall}}}}{=}{L}_{D}(\rho_{D}).

In the above display, step (a) follows by observing that
\rho_{D}\leq\rho_{D+1}
. Step (b) relies on the observation that
\mathcal{P}_{D}\subset\mathcal{P}_{D+1}
and the infimum over larger sets is smaller. Hence
({L}_{D}(\rho_{D}))_{D\in\mathbb{N}}
is a non-increasing sequence. To show that
{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})\downarrow{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
, we begin by observing that by repeating the argument used in the previous display, we can also obtain:

\displaystyle{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})\geq{L}_{\infty}(\rho).

Hence, we have obtained a lower bound for
{L}_{D}({\rho_{D}})
in terms of
{L}_{\infty}({\rho})
. To show that in fact
{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})\downarrow{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
we will also need an upper bound. Towards this goal, we consider a sequence of functions
\{\widehat{\Psi}_{D}\}_{D\in\mathbb{N}}
indexed by
D\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\widehat{\Psi}_{D}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is a degree-
D
polynomial and:

\begin{split}&\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\|\widehat{\Psi}_{D}-{\Psi_{\star}}(\cdot;\rho)\|_{\mu+\nu}^{2}=0\\
\Leftrightarrow&\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}|\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\mathsf{\Lambda})-{\Psi_{\star}}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho)|^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}|\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-{\Psi_{\star}}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu});\rho)|^{2}\right)=0.\end{split}

(220)

The existence of these polynomial approximations follows by the fact that polynomials form a dense subset of
L^{2}(\mu+\nu)
since
\mu+\nu
is a compactly supported measure
[
71
, Corollary 14.24, Definition 14.1]
. We can use the function
\lambda\mapsto\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\lambda)-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]
as a candidate minimizer to upper bound
{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})
:

\displaystyle{L}_{\infty}(\rho)

\displaystyle\leq{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\right|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{\rho_{D}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\mathsf{\Lambda})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\Psi}_{D}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]\right|^{2}\right]

We let
D\rightarrow\infty
in the above display and exploit (
220
) and
\rho_{D}\rightarrow\rho\in(0,\infty)
to conclude that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu};\rho)-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho)]={L}_{\infty}(\rho),

as claimed. Finally, we show that:

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\mu}|{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho_{D})-{\Psi_{\star}}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho)|^{2}

\displaystyle=0,\quad\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu}\sim\nu}|{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-{\Psi_{\star}}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu});\rho_{D})|^{2}=0

To do so, we will exploit the strong convexity of the objective function:

\displaystyle{O}(\Psi)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})].

(221)

Notice that
{O}(\Psi)-\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]
is convex and hence for any
\lambda\in(0,1)
and any
\Psi,\widehat{\Psi}\in L^{2}(\mu+\nu)
,

\displaystyle{O}(\lambda\Psi+(1-\lambda)\widehat{\Psi})-\mathbb{E}[|\lambda\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})+(1-\lambda)\widehat{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\lambda{O}(\Psi)+(1-\lambda){O}(\widehat{\Psi})-\lambda\mathbb{E}[|\Psi(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]-(1-\lambda)\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}].

Rearranging yields:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-\widehat{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})|^{2}]\leq\frac{\lambda{O}(\Psi)+(1-\lambda){O}(\widehat{\Psi})-{O}(\lambda\Psi+(1-\lambda)\Psi)}{\lambda(1-\lambda)}.

We instantiate the above inequality with
\lambda=1/2
,
\Psi=\Psi_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho)
and
\widehat{\Psi}={\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho_{D})
. For notational convenience, we define the “midpoint” between these two matrix denoisers as
\Phi^{(D)}\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}(\Psi_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho)+{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho_{D}))/2
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-\widehat{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot\left[{{O}(\Psi_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho))+{O}({\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho_{D}))-2{O}(\Phi^{(D)})}\right]

(222)

Recalling that
\Psi_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho)
is the minimizer of the variational problem that defines
{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
(claim 2 of the lemma):

\displaystyle{O}(\Psi_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:scrO}}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[|\Psi_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Psi_{\star}^{2}(\mathsf{\Lambda})]={L}_{\infty}(\rho).

(223)

Moreover, since
\Phi^{(D)}
is a feasible point for the optimization problem that defines
{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
(recall (
218
)), we conclude that:

\displaystyle{O}(\Phi^{(D)})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:scrO}}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[|\Phi^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\Phi^{(D)}(\mathsf{\Lambda})^{2}]\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:scrL-limit-repara}}}}{\geq}{L}_{\infty}(\rho).

(224)

Recall that
\rho_{D}\uparrow\rho
and that
{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho_{D})
is the minimizer of the variational problem that defines
{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})
. Hence,

\displaystyle{O}({\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\cdot\;;\rho_{D}))

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{\eqref{eq:scrO}}}}{=}\mathbb{E}[|{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu};\rho_{D})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho_{D})^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[|{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu};\rho_{D})-1|^{2}]+\frac{1}{\rho_{D}}\cdot\mathbb{E}[{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho_{D})^{2}]={L}_{D}(\rho_{D}).

(225)

Plugging in (
223
), (
224
), and (
225
) into (
222
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-\widehat{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot({L}_{D}(\rho_{D})-{L}_{\infty}(\rho)).

We have already shown that
{L}_{D}(\rho_{D})\rightarrow{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
as
D\rightarrow\infty
. Hence,

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[|{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})-\widehat{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda}_{\nu})|^{2}]=0.

(226)

An analogous argument yields
\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[|{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda})-\widehat{\Psi}(\mathsf{\Lambda})|^{2}]=0
, which completes the proof of Claim (4) in the statement of the lemma.

Proof of Claim (5)

Recall that from the first claim of the lemma:

\displaystyle 1-\mathbb{E}_{\mathsf{\Lambda}\sim\nu}[{\Psi}^{(D)}_{\star}(\mathsf{\Lambda};\rho)]={L}_{D}(\rho).

Taking
D\rightarrow\infty
and using the fact that
{L}_{D}(\rho)\rightarrow{L}_{\infty}(\rho)
and (
226
) yields the desired conclusion. This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Appendix E
Equivalence of State Evolution and Replica Fixed Point Equations (Proposition
2
)

This appendix is devoted to proving Proposition
2
. We begin with some preliminary results about the trace ensemble. The proof of Proposition
2
will be presented in Appendix
E.2
.

E.1
Preliminary Results on the Trace Ensemble

We remind the reader that under the trace ensemble, the noise matrix
\bm{W}
is drawn from the probability density function:

\displaystyle p(\bm{W})

\displaystyle\propto\exp\left(-\frac{N}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{N}V(\lambda_{i}(\bm{W}))\right)\text{ where $\lambda_{1:N}(\bm{W})$ denote the eigenvalues of $\bm{W}$,}

(227)

and
V:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
denotes the potential function, which is a functional parameter for the noise model. In addition, we recall the definition of the function
\phi:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
(introduced in Lemma
1
), which plays an important role in this paper:

\displaystyle\phi(\lambda)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}{(1-\pi\theta{H}_{\mu}(\lambda))^{2}+\pi^{2}\theta^{2}\mu^{2}(\lambda)}\quad\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R}.

(228)

In the above display,
{H}_{\mu}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is the Hilbert transform of
\mu
, the limiting spectral measure of the noise matrix
\bm{W}
. Lemma
17
below summarizes some useful properties of the trace ensemble.

Lemma 17

(The function
\phi(\lambda)
for trace ensemble)
.

Suppose that the noise matrix
\bm{W}
is drawn from the trace ensemble defined in (
227
). Assume that the potential
V:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
is a degree-
k
polynomial for some
k\geq 2
. Let
\mu
denote the limiting spectral measure of
\bm{W}
and let
\phi:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
be the function from (
228
). Then:

1.

The spectral measure
\mu
is absolutely continuous with density function:

\mu(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sqrt{\big(4Q(\lambda)-(V^{\prime}(\lambda))^{2}\big)_{+}},\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R},

(229)

where
(x)_{+}=\max\{x,0\}
and
Q(\lambda)
is given by the Cauchy principal value integral:

Q(\lambda)=\int\frac{V^{\prime}(\lambda)-V^{\prime}(t)}{\lambda-t}\mu(t)\mathrm{d}t.

(230)

The support of
\mu
is a finite union of finite intervals, and the density function
\mu(\lambda)
is Holder continuous.

2.

Define
{\phi}_{\text{poly}}:\mathbb{R}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
as

\begin{split}{\phi}_{\text{poly}}(\lambda)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}1-\theta\cdot V^{\prime}(\lambda)+\theta^{2}\cdot Q(\lambda),\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\mathbb{R}.\end{split}

(231)

Then,
{\phi}_{\text{poly}}
is a degree
k-1
polynomial and

\displaystyle\phi(\lambda)={\phi}_{\text{poly}}(\lambda)\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\text{Supp}(\mu),

(232)

\displaystyle\phi(\lambda)={\phi}_{\text{poly}}(\lambda)\quad\forall\;\lambda\;\in\;\text{Supp}(\nu)

(233)

Proof.

We consider the two claims one by one.

1.

The expression of the density function of
\mu
can be found in, e.g.,
[
61
, Theorem 11.2.1]
. Since
V(x)
is a degree-
k
polynomial,
V^{\prime}(\lambda)
is a degree
k-1
polynomial and
Q(\lambda)
defined in (
230
) is a degree
k-2
polynomial. Hence, the support
\text{Supp}(\mu)=\left\{\lambda|4Q(\lambda)-(V^{\prime}(\lambda))^{2}\geq 0\right\}
must be a union of finite intervals
[
61
, Eq. (11.2.14)]
. Furthermore,
\mu(\lambda)
is a composition of Holder continuous functions and is therefore Holder continuous.

2.

To prove the second claim, we note that the Hilbert transform of
\mu(\lambda)
is related to the potential function via
[
61
, Theorem 11.2.1]
:

\pi\cdot{H}_{\mu}(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2}V^{\prime}(\lambda),\quad\forall\lambda\in\text{Supp}(\mu).

(234)

Substituting (
229
) and (
234
) into (
228
), we get

\begin{split}\phi(\lambda)&\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\left[1-\theta\pi{H}_{\mu}(\lambda)\right]^{2}+\pi^{2}\theta^{2}\mu^{2}\left(\lambda\right)\\
&=1-\theta\cdot V^{\prime}(\lambda)+\theta^{2}\cdot Q(\lambda)\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\phi_{\text{poly}}(\lambda)\quad\forall\lambda\in\text{Supp}(\mu).\end{split}

(235)

This verifies (
232
). To show that (
233
), we consider the Lebesgue decomposition of
\nu=\nu_{\parallel}+\nu_{\perp}
into the absolutely continuous part
\nu_{\parallel}
and the singular part
\nu_{\perp}
. Recall from Lemma
1
that
\nu_{\parallel}
has the same support as
\mu
. Hence, we only need to show that
\phi=\phi_{\text{poly}}
on
\text{Supp}(\nu_{\perp})
. Lemma
1
shows that the measure
\nu_{\perp}
concentrates on the set
S\overset{\text{\tiny{def}}}{=}\{x\in\mathbb{R}:\phi(x)=0\}
. It is therefore sufficient to prove
\phi_{\text{poly}}(\lambda)=\phi(\lambda)
for
\lambda\in S
. From (
228
), we have

\pi{H}_{\mu}(\lambda)=\frac{1}{\theta}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu(\lambda)=0,\quad\forall\lambda\in S.
